## FILE: llms.txt  (2,530 bytes)

```cmake
# rag-cpp

> Header-only C++20 hybrid RAG engine (dense + BM25 + RRF, SIMD, zero-allocation queries, lock-free concurrent commits, crash-safe persistence, PDF ingestion with page citations) plus a self-learning RL agent (tile-coded Q-learning, actor-critic, a model-based planner that checks its own model) with held-out evaluation, champion/challenger deployment, two-stage drift detection, a policy bank and a baseline guard, benchmarked against an exact dynamic-programming optimum.

## Docs
- [Hand-off pack: start here](docs/ai/README.md): reading order and ground rules
- [Objectives](docs/ai/OBJECTIVES.md): goals, success criteria, non-goals, request history
- [Specs](docs/ai/SPECS.md): toolchain, libraries and versions, build options, machine, CI matrix
- [Task specs](docs/ai/TASK_SPECS.md): exact environment dynamics, rewards, optima, learner updates, metrics
- [Project map](docs/ai/PROJECT_MAP.md): files, dependency layers, build targets, CI
- [API cheat sheet](docs/ai/API.md): compiled examples for the engine, PDFs, persistence, batching, agent
- [RAG engine internals](docs/ai/RAG_ENGINE.md): data flow, invariants, concurrency, file format, performance
- [Agent architecture](docs/agent.md): environment, encoders, learners, supervision, lessons
- [ES order-flow trading](docs/ai/ORDERFLOW.md): simulator, scalping and tick-scalping agents, backtester, calibration, results OF1-OF9, plan
- [Next session](docs/ai/NEXT_SESSION.md): the prompt for a new Claude Code session, its agent architecture and workflow template, seed ledger, red flags
- [Research synthesis](experiments/research_synthesis.md): what 595 crawled papers say about the order-flow work, ranked changes
- [Windows build](docs/WINDOWS.md): Visual Studio 2022 / MSVC build, test and run steps, optimization flags
- [Lessons](docs/ai/LESSONS.md): 27 cross-cutting lessons from the experiments, each with its evidence
- [Research notes](docs/ai/RESEARCH.md): literature review (noisy-target RL, execution, market making) with ranked next experiments
- [Experiment log](docs/ai/EXPERIMENTS.md): RL experiments E0-E28 with results, including failures (summary table at the top)
- [Decision log](docs/ai/DECISIONS.md): design decisions with evidence
- [Conventions](docs/ai/CONVENTIONS.md): coding, testing and verification rules
- [Status](docs/ai/STATUS.md): limitations and next steps

## Optional
- [README](README.md): overview with benchmark tables
- [Single-file bundle generator](tools/make_context_pack.py): writes dist/context_pack_*.md

```

## FILE: docs/ai/README.md  (2,825 bytes)

# Hand-off pack for an AI assistant

This folder describes rag-cpp for a language model (or a person) taking over
the project. It's written to be pasted into a model's context, either as
separate files or as the single bundle produced by:

```bash
python3 tools/make_context_pack.py                 # docs only  -> dist/context_pack_docs.md
python3 tools/make_context_pack.py --with-source   # docs + all headers, tests, examples -> dist/context_pack_full.md
```

## Reading order

1. `OBJECTIVES.md`: what the project is for, success criteria, non-goals,
   and the requests it grew from.
2. `PROJECT_MAP.md`: what exists, where, and how it's built.
3. `SPECS.md`: toolchain, libraries (with versions), build options, the
   measurement machine, the CI matrix.
4. `API.md`: how to use it. Every snippet compiles (`tests/api_examples_tests.cpp`).
5. `RAG_ENGINE.md`: engine internals, invariants, file format.
6. `../agent.md`: the self-learning agent's architecture.
7. `TASK_SPECS.md`: exact task dynamics, rewards, optima, learner update
   rules and metrics.
8. `LESSONS.md`: the experiments' cross-cutting lessons, one paragraph each.
   `RESEARCH.md`: a literature review mapping published RL results onto
   them, with ranked next experiments.
9. `EXPERIMENTS.md`: what was tried with the agent and what happened.
10. `DECISIONS.md`: why things are the way they are, with evidence.
11. `CONVENTIONS.md`: rules to follow when changing code.
12. `STATUS.md`: limitations and next steps.
13. `ORDERFLOW.md`: **the ES order-flow trading work** (simulator, two
    agents, backtester, calibration, results OF1–OF9, ranked plan). Start
    here to continue the trading work; `../../experiments/research_synthesis.md`
    maps the literature onto it.
14. `NEXT_SESSION.md`: the prompt to start a new Claude Code session with,
    and how that session should organise its agents (roles, file
    ownership, a workflow template, the seed ledger, red flags).
15. `OF16_REAL_ES_PLAN.md`: the pre-registered plan for scoring the OF15
    agents on the user's recorded ES book and trades (day manifest,
    statistics, decision rule). Plan only until its phase 2 is run.

## Ground rules for an AI working on this code

* **Measure, don't assume.** Every number in these docs came from a run.
  If you change code, re-measure before quoting.
* **Run the checks.** Run the full test matrix (CONVENTIONS.md) before
  claiming something works. The gate is the full MSVC build and
  test suite on the user's Windows server (CI runs `msvc-windows` only).
* **Keep the guarantees.** Hot paths must not allocate. Batched queries are
  bit-identical to single ones. Loaders validate every field. Tests enforce
  all three.
* **Document negative results.** Record failed experiments in
  EXPERIMENTS.md; they're as useful as the successes.

## FILE: docs/ai/OBJECTIVES.md  (6,464 bytes)

# Objectives

What this project is for, what "done" means for each part, and what is
deliberately out of scope. The history at the end records how the
objectives grew, so a new contributor can tell requirements from
experiments.

## 1. The RAG engine

**Goal:** a header-only C++20 retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) engine
written the way low-latency trading systems are written: predictable tail
latency, no allocation on the hot path, explicit memory layout, and
measured rather than assumed performance.

Functional requirements:

* Ingest documents (plain text, source files, PDFs with page numbers),
  chunk them with overlap, embed and index them.
* Hybrid retrieval: dense vector search plus BM25, fused by reciprocal rank
  fusion, with four dense modes (exact, int8 + rerank, IVF, IVF-SQ8).
* Build a prompt into a fixed buffer, with citations (`[n] (source, p. X)`),
  ready for any LLM (the generator itself is a concept, not a dependency).
* Persist an index to disk and load it back, safely.

Non-functional requirements and how they're enforced:

| Requirement | Target | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|
| No heap allocation on the query path | 0 allocations per query | global `operator new` counter in tests (R2) |
| Concurrency | queries concurrent with ingest and commit | TSan job, `rag_stress` (R7) |
| Batched queries | bit-identical to single queries | tests (R6) |
| Crash safety | saves survive power loss; forged files are rejected | fsync + rename + directory fsync (R10); structural validation and forgery tests (R9) |
| Robustness | no UB or memory errors on any input | ASan/UBSan (GCC and Clang), libFuzzer harnesses |
| Latency | sub-millisecond p99 at 60k chunks | IVF-SQ8: p99 0.43 ms (R5; README) |

## 2. The self-learning agent

**Goal:** a self-learning agent whose architecture can be studied piece
by piece (environment, encoder, learners, exploration, supervision), on
tasks where the optimum is known exactly, so every claim is a measurement.

Objectives, in the order they were added:

1. Learn optimal execution (sell 100 lots in 20 steps with a predictive
   signal and market impact) from rewards alone, scored against an exact
   DP optimum.
2. Supervise learning the way production systems need: held-out
   evaluation, champion/challenger deployment, drift detection and
   re-exploration, a policy bank for returning regimes, and a guard against
   deploying anything worse than a simple baseline.
3. Compare learning paradigms on the same task: tile-coded Q-learning,
   episodic memory, actor-critic, and a model-based agent that learns the
   market and plans, including what happens when the model is wrong and how
   to detect it.
4. Show the architecture is task-agnostic: a second task (market making)
   on which the unchanged agent finds the optimal policy.
5. Measure the things means hide: online cost of learning, tail risk,
   off-policy evaluation from logs.

What "success" means for the agent: each experiment answers a stated
question with numbers, across seeds, against the exact optimum, and records
the result even when it's negative (EXPERIMENTS.md). Library defaults
change only when an experiment shows they should (DECISIONS.md).

## 3. ES order-flow trading (current focus)

**Goal:** RL agents that trade ES futures from order flow, validated to
a trading desk's standard before any real money. Two strategies:
order-flow scalping/momentum (market orders) and tick scalping (limit
orders, queue position). Develop on a realistic simulator now; calibrate
to and backtest on the user's ES tick data next; train on the user's
many-core server. Success criteria, standard of evidence, status and the
ranked plan: `ORDERFLOW.md`.

## 4. The hand-off pack

**Goal:** another AI, or a person, can take over the project from these
documents alone.

* `llms.txt` and `docs/ai/` describe everything; `tools/make_context_pack.py`
  bundles them into one file (optionally with every source file).
* Every API snippet in API.md compiles and runs as a test.
* The pack is kept current after every experiment and pushed with it.

## Non-goals

* A neural embedding model or an LLM client. Both are concepts
  (`Embedder`, `Generator`) so they can be plugged in; none could be
  downloaded and tested in this environment.
* Distributed or multi-node operation; deletes and updates of indexed
  chunks (STATUS.md lists these as limitations).
* A realistic market simulator. Both trading environments are stylized on
  purpose: they keep an exact optimum computable, which is what makes the
  agent's numbers meaningful. Real market data would make the price-impact
  model an untestable assumption.
* GPU support.

## How the objectives grew (the requests, in order)

| Request | What it produced |
|---|---|
| "Make a custom C++ RAG, with high performance snippets and architecture" | the engine (`include/rag/`) |
| "Make the repo" | https://github.com/Rcoding123/rag-cpp |
| "Can you test it?", then "keep building", "keep doing more tests" | CI matrix, concurrency, IVF-SQ8, persistence, batching, coverage, fuzzing, forgery, differential and stress tests |
| "Make notes on PDFs", "add PDF support" | Poppler extraction, text cleanup, page citations, the `rag_pdf` CLI |
| "Make a self-learning agent; I want to learn the architecture" | `include/rag/agent/`, `docs/agent.md` |
| "Keep experimenting with the RL… keep programming, testing and simulating" | experiments E1–E14 (EXPERIMENTS.md) |
| "Create documents, layouts etc. along with the RAG engine so I can feed all of it to another AI" | this hand-off pack |
| "Make the documents: the specs you're using, the libraries, objectives" | SPECS.md, OBJECTIVES.md, TASK_SPECS.md |
| "I need to run this on Windows too", "make sure it's optimized" | MSVC build, presets (AVX2, AVX-512), `docs/WINDOWS.md`; verified 285/285 on the user's Ryzen 9 9900X |
| "Aim for a proper trading strategy and validation" | `validation.hpp` (stress suite, sealed seeds), E28 |
| "ES futures… directional trading… order flow… two agents: scalping/momentum and tick scalping (queue, OFI, order arrival)" | `include/rag/orderflow/`, OF1–OF9, `ORDERFLOW.md` |
| "Start a web crawler for RL trading papers; make it anonymous" | `tools/research_crawler.py`, `experiments/research_synthesis.md` |
| "Make the code suitable for my server (and rented GPUs); the next AI should pick up the work" | `of_train` (parallel seeds), parallel calibration, `ORDERFLOW.md` §3 and §7 |

## FILE: docs/ai/PROJECT_MAP.md  (12,750 bytes)

# Project map

rag-cpp is a header-only C++20 library with two parts:

1. **A RAG engine** (`include/rag/`): hybrid retrieval (dense vectors + BM25,
   fused by reciprocal rank fusion) with a zero-allocation query path, SIMD
   kernels, lock-free concurrent ingestion and commits, persistence, and PDF
   ingestion with page citations.
2. **A self-learning agent** (`include/rag/agent/`): reinforcement learning
   with held-out evaluation, champion/challenger deployment and drift
   detection. It is demonstrated on an optimal-execution task whose exact
   optimum is computed by dynamic programming.

## Repository layout

```
rag-cpp/
├── include/
│   ├── rag.hpp                  umbrella header: #include "rag/engine.hpp"
│   └── rag/
│       ├── common.hpp           kCacheLine, AlignedArray (2 MiB huge-page alignment, Init::Prefault/Lazy), ValidDim<D>
│       ├── arena.hpp            bump allocator; blocks never move (stable string_views for readers)
│       ├── tokenizer.hpp        constexpr byte LUT + FNV-1a hashing; constexpr stopword table
│       ├── chunker.hpp          sliding token windows -> byte ranges (zero-copy chunks)
│       ├── embedder.hpp         Embedder concept, HashingEmbedder<D>, embedder_tag<E>()
│       ├── simd.hpp             dot (fp32), dot4 (4 rows), dot1 (one dot4 lane, bit-identical), dot_i8 (AVX-512 VNNI/AVX2/scalar), quantize_i8
│       ├── topk.hpp             TopK<Cap>: fixed-capacity heap, cached admission threshold, min-score floor
│       ├── vector_store.hpp     SoA fp32 + int8 rows, single-writer/multi-reader publication, batched scans
│       ├── ivf_index.hpp        IVF-Flat / IVF-SQ8 (spherical k-means, CSR lists), save/load with validation
│       ├── bm25.hpp             Bm25Builder -> frozen Bm25Index with precomputed per-posting impacts
│       ├── fusion.hpp           reciprocal rank fusion
│       ├── prompt.hpp           ChunkView, citation formatting, prompt assembly into a fixed buffer
│       ├── epoch.hpp            epoch-based reclamation (per-reader padded slots)
│       ├── io.hpp               checksummed binary I/O, bounds-checked reads, fsync + atomic rename
│       ├── engine.hpp           RagEngine: ingest / commit / query / query_batch / save / load / inspect
│       ├── text_clean.hpp       PDF-text cleanup: ligatures, dehyphenation, running headers/footers, reflow
│       ├── pdf.hpp              (optional, Poppler) PDF extraction + ingest_pdf()
│       └── agent/
│           ├── rng.hpp          xoshiro256** + splitmix seeding, Marsaglia normals
│           ├── env.hpp          Environment concept, ChainEnv, ExecutionEnv (costs, risk aversion)
│           ├── execution_dp.hpp exact optimum for ExecutionEnv (cost tables, policy_value, evaluate_policy)
│           ├── features.hpp     TileCoder, RandomFourierEncoder
│           ├── learners.hpp     TileQ, ReplayBuffer, EpisodicMemory
│           ├── mlp.hpp          from-scratch MLP: backprop, Adam, SGD (for NeuralQ)
│           ├── policy_table.hpp compile a policy into a lookup table; save/load; median-vote ensembles (E22, E23)
│           ├── validation.hpp   stress suite, sealed test seeds, paired trading metrics (E28)
│       └── orderflow/           ES order-flow trading (ORDERFLOW.md)
│           ├── simulator.hpp    EsSimulator: tick-level ES book, takers, meta-orders, fading, icebergs, FIFO own orders
│           ├── features.hpp     FeatureBuilder: 13 order-flow features from any tick stream
│           ├── scalp_env.hpp    ScalpEnv: directional scalping with market orders
│           ├── tick_env.hpp     TickScalpEnv: passive tick scalping with queue position
│           ├── tick_csv.hpp     recorded ticks from CSV, bucketed
│           ├── replay_env.hpp   TickTape + ReplayScalpEnv: backtests on recorded ticks
│           ├── seed_ledger.hpp  the seed-block table + process-role guard (forbid_sealed, open_sealed; OF15, A71)
│           ├── vec_scalp_env.hpp Pool, EpisodeSource, VecScalpEnv, VecReplayEnv, run_builtin (batched ScalpEnv; OF15)
│           ├── stylized.hpp     stylized facts of a tick stream (calibration)
│           ├── agent.hpp        Agent<Env>: TileQ / Episodic / ActorCritic / NeuralQ, n-step, distill, snapshots
│           ├── evaluate.hpp     evaluate / compare on common random numbers (EvalStats)
│           ├── drift.hpp        DriftDetector: one-sided CUSUM on surprise (DriftParams)
│           ├── ope.hpp          off-policy evaluation: PDIS, WPDIS, doubly robust, ESS diagnostics
│           ├── variance_reduction.hpp  InnovationControlledEnv: control variate for the price noise
│           ├── tuning.hpp       (optional, NLopt) maximize / maximize_global over log/integer parameters
│           ├── analysis.hpp     (optional, Eigen) least squares, quadratic sensitivity
│           ├── model_based.hpp  ModelBasedExecutionAgent: regression + DP planning + check_model
│           ├── market_making.hpp a second task: MarketMakingEnv, exact MarketMakingDP, ModelBasedMarketMaker
│           └── trainer.hpp      evaluate, compare, DriftDetector (CUSUM), Trainer (champion, bank, baseline guard)
├── tests/
│   ├── rag_tests.cpp            engine unit/integration tests; global operator-new counter
│   ├── forgery_tests.cpp        every loader check, against files with valid checksums
│   ├── reference_tests.cpp      differential tests vs a naive spec-level reference

```

## Dependency layers (lower layers never include higher ones)

```
L0  common.hpp  rng.hpp
L1  arena  tokenizer  simd  topk  io  text_clean
L2  chunker  embedder  vector_store  bm25  fusion  prompt  epoch  features
L3  ivf_index                    learners      env  market_making  mlp
L4  engine                       execution_dp  agent  evaluate  drift  ope
L5  pdf (Poppler)                trainer       model_based
```

The agent reuses the engine's SIMD kernels (`simd.hpp`), top-K selection
(`topk.hpp`) and aligned buffers (`common.hpp`). It doesn't depend on
`engine.hpp`.

## Build targets

| Target | What | Needs |
|---|---|---|
| `rag_tests` | main test binary (rag, forgery, reference, text_clean, agent, model_based, market_making, mlp, api_examples) | GoogleTest |
| `rag_pdf_tests` | PDF tests | GoogleTest, poppler-cpp |
| `rag_stress` | concurrency stress test (also registered with ctest, 2 s per mode) | threads |
| `rag_bench` | microbenchmarks | Google Benchmark |
| `rag_latency` | p50/p99/p99.9 query latency | |
| `rag_demo`, `agent_demo`, `rag_pdf` | examples and CLI | `rag_pdf` needs poppler-cpp |
| `rag_optlib_tests`, `tune_agent` | tuning/analysis tests and tool | NLopt, Eigen |
| `fuzz_load`, `fuzz_text`, `make_seeds` | with `-DRAG_FUZZ=ON` and Clang | libclang-rt |
| `_ragof`, `of15_refdump`; ctest `ragrl_pytest`, `ragrl_smoke_*` (9) | with `-DRAG_PYTHON=ON`: the Python module, its reference dumper and the ragrl tests | `.venv` (Python 3.12, nanobind) |

CMake options: `RAG_ARCH` (`-march` value, default `native`), `RAG_SANITIZE`
(ASan+UBSan), `RAG_TSAN`, `RAG_HUGEPAGES` (default ON), `RAG_FUZZ`, `RAG_PDF`
(default ON; used if Poppler is found), `RAG_PYTHON` (default OFF; the
`_ragof` module and the ragrl tests, A72).

## CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`)

* **Test matrix:** gcc native / x86-64-v3 (AVX2) / x86-64 (scalar), clang
  native, gcc ASan+UBSan, clang ASan+UBSan, gcc TSan (concurrency tests
  repeated 25 times plus `rag_stress 10`). All build with `-Werror`.
* **fuzz-smoke:** each fuzzer for 60 s.

## FILE: docs/ai/SPECS.md  (7,665 bytes)

# Specs: toolchain, machine, libraries, build

Everything here was read off the machine and the build files, not
remembered. If you rebuild elsewhere, re-measure before quoting numbers
(see CONVENTIONS.md).

## Language and build

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Language | C++20 (`CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD 20`, required, no GNU extensions) |
| Library form | header-only: `include/rag/` (engine) and `include/rag/agent/` (agent); CMake `INTERFACE` target `rag` |
| Build system | CMake ≥ 3.20 (measured with 3.28.3); default build type `Release` (`-O3 -DNDEBUG`) |
| Warnings | `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Wconversion -Wno-sign-conversion`; CI adds `-Werror` |
| ISA | `-march=${RAG_ARCH}`, default `native`; CI also builds `x86-64-v3` (AVX2) and `x86-64` (scalar) |
| SIMD paths | AVX-512 (+VNNI for int8), AVX2 + FMA, scalar fallback; chosen at compile time |

CMake options:

| Option | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `RAG_ARCH` | `native` | value of `-march` |
| `RAG_SANITIZE` | OFF | ASan + UBSan (`-fsanitize=address,undefined`) |
| `RAG_TSAN` | OFF | ThreadSanitizer |
| `RAG_HUGEPAGES` | ON | transparent huge pages for large arrays (`MADV_HUGEPAGE`) |
| `RAG_FUZZ` | OFF | libFuzzer targets (Clang only) |
| `RAG_PDF` | ON | PDF support, used only if poppler-cpp is found |
| `RAG_OPTLIBS` | ON | NLopt/Eigen tuning and analysis targets, used only if both are found |

## Toolchains used

| Tool | Version |
|---|---|
| GCC | 13.3.0 (Ubuntu 13.3.0-6ubuntu2~24.04.1) |
| Clang | 18.1.3 (Ubuntu 1ubuntu1) |
| CMake | 3.28.3 |
| Python (tools only) | 3.13 |
| OS | Ubuntu 24.04.5 LTS, Linux 6.18 |
| CI runners | GitHub Actions `ubuntu-24.04`; `windows-2022` for MSVC |
| MSVC (Windows) | Visual Studio 2022 (17.x), `/O2 /arch:AVX2`, via `CMakePresets.json` (see `docs/WINDOWS.md`) |

## Libraries

The engine and the agent use only the C++ standard library, compiler
intrinsics (`<immintrin.h>`) and a few OS calls behind `#if`: on Linux
`madvise` (huge pages) and `fsync`; on Windows `_aligned_malloc` and
`_commit`. Everything else is optional and used for testing,
benchmarking or PDF input.

| Library | Version measured | Ubuntu package | Used by | Required? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C++ standard library (libstdc++) | GCC 13 | — | everything | yes |
| GoogleTest | 1.14.0 | `libgtest-dev` | `rag_tests`, `rag_pdf_tests` | for tests |
| Google Benchmark | 1.8.3 | `libbenchmark-dev` | `rag_bench` | optional (skipped if absent) |
| poppler-cpp | 24.02.0 | `libpoppler-cpp-dev` | `rag/pdf.hpp`, `rag_pdf`, `rag_pdf_tests` | optional (`RAG_PDF`) |
| libFuzzer + sanitizer runtimes | LLVM 18 | `libclang-rt-18-dev` | `fuzz_load`, `fuzz_text` | optional (`RAG_FUZZ`) |
| NLopt | 2.7.1 | `libnlopt-cxx-dev` | `rag/agent/tuning.hpp`, `tune_agent`, `rag_optlib_tests` | optional (`RAG_OPTLIBS`) |
| Eigen | 3.4.0 | `libeigen3-dev` | `rag/agent/analysis.hpp`, `tune_agent`, `rag_optlib_tests` | optional (`RAG_OPTLIBS`) |
| pthreads | glibc | — | concurrency tests, `rag_stress` | yes (via `Threads::Threads`) |

Install on Ubuntu 24.04:

```bash
sudo apt-get install -y cmake g++ libgtest-dev libbenchmark-dev libpoppler-cpp-dev libeigen3-dev libnlopt-cxx-dev
```

Not used, on purpose: no BLAS, no Eigen, no Boost, no JSON library, no
neural-network runtime. The embedder is a concept; `HashingEmbedder` is a
dependency-free stand-in (feature-hashed unigrams and bigrams). An ONNX or
llama.cpp encoder can be plugged in behind the same concept (STATUS.md).

### No RL library

The reinforcement-learning stack is written from scratch in
`include/rag/agent/`; no Gymnasium, Stable-Baselines3, RLlib, PyTorch or
other framework is used. What each piece corresponds to:

| Piece | Header | Closest off-the-shelf equivalent |
|---|---|---|
| `Environment` concept (`reset`, `step`, `features`, action count, feature ranges) | `env.hpp` | Gymnasium `Env` |
| Tile coding, random Fourier features | `features.hpp` | Sutton's tile coder; scikit-learn `RBFSampler` |
| Q-learning (n-step, replay, annealed step size), episodic k-NN memory | `learners.hpp`, `agent.hpp` | SB3 DQN (with a linear model instead of a network) |
| Neural Q-learner: MLP, backprop, Adam, target network (`NeuralQ`) | `mlp.hpp`, `agent.hpp` | SB3 DQN / PyTorch |
| Policy compilation to a lookup table, ensembles by vote | `policy_table.hpp` | (none standard) |
| Actor-critic (softmax policy, TD critic) | `agent.hpp` | SB3 A2C |
| Model-based agent (regression + DP planning) | `model_based.hpp` | none standard |
| Exact optima by dynamic programming | `execution_dp.hpp`, `market_making.hpp` | none (task-specific) |
| Training loop, held-out evaluation, champion, drift detection, policy bank, baseline guard | `trainer.hpp`, `drift.hpp` | partly RLlib / SB3 callbacks |
| Paired evaluation, CVaR | `evaluate.hpp` | none standard |
| Off-policy evaluation (PDIS, WPDIS, DR, ESS) | `ope.hpp` | SCOPE-RL, d3rlpy OPE |

Why from scratch:
* **Latency:** a greedy decision costs 64 ns (TileQ) with zero heap
  allocations, which a Python framework can't approach.
* **Transparency:** each update rule is a few lines (TASK_SPECS.md §5),
  which matters because the agent is meant for learning the architecture.
* **Reproducibility:** every random draw comes from seeded generators, so
  experiments reproduce bit for bit, and paired comparisons (common random
  numbers) are exact.
* **Exact yardsticks:** the tasks are built so a DP can compute the optimum;
  that integration is simplest when everything shares one codebase.

The trade-off: the neural network is small and CPU-only (an MLP in double
precision, no GPU, no automatic differentiation beyond the hand-written
backpropagation), and there are no vectorized environments or distributed
training. E16 tested whether a framework-style neural learner would help:
the from-scratch MLP did (84–89% vs 65–69% on execution).

## Measurement machine

All performance numbers in these docs come from one shared cloud VM:

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| CPU | Intel Xeon @ 2.10 GHz, 4 vCPUs, 1 thread per core |
| ISA features | AVX2, FMA, AVX-512F, AVX-512 VNNI |
| Caches | L1d 48 KiB per core, L2 2 MiB per core, L3 260 MiB shared |
| Memory | 15 GiB |
| Kernel | Linux 6.18 |

A shared VM is noisy: timing differences under ~10% aren't meaningful
unless they reproduce.

## Test and CI matrix

Windows only since 2026-11-04 (the user's decision). The authoritative gate
is the full AVX-512 build and `ctest` on the user's server; GitHub CI keeps
one job. The Linux jobs (GCC native/AVX2/scalar, Clang, ASan+UBSan with
both compilers, TSan, libFuzzer smoke) were removed; `e0a4c7a`'s `ci.yml`
has them.

| Job | Compiler | Flags | What it checks |
|---|---|---|---|
| user's server (local gate) | MSVC | Release, AVX-512 preset | full build, full test suite |
| msvc-windows (GitHub) | MSVC (VS 2022) | Release, `/arch:AVX2` | Windows build, full test suite, demos, latency probe, AVX-512 compile |

`ctest` registers every GoogleTest case plus `stress_all_modes` (the
concurrent stress run) and `rag_pdf_cli_smoke`. STATUS.md has the current
count.

## Numeric conventions

* Embeddings: `float` (fp32), L2-normalized; int8 copies with one scale per
  row.
* Agent: `double` throughout; actions are `std::uint32_t`.
* Randomness: xoshiro256** seeded through splitmix64 (`rng.hpp`);
  normal variates by the Marsaglia polar method. Every experiment is
  deterministic given its seeds; evaluation episode i always uses
  `Rng(seed + i)`.
* Floating point: results are bit-reproducible on one build. Across ISAs
  and compilers, FMA contraction can change the last bits, so tests assert
  tolerances or directions, not exact learned decisions.

## FILE: docs/ai/API.md  (18,715 bytes)

# API cheat sheet

Every snippet here is compiled and run by `tests/api_examples_tests.cpp`. If
you change an API, update both files.

## 1. Index and query

```cpp
#include "rag.hpp"
using Engine = rag::RagEngine<rag::HashingEmbedder<384>>;

rag::EngineConfig cfg;
cfg.max_chunks = 100'000;                       // capacity; memory is committed lazily
cfg.chunker = {.window_tokens = 128, .overlap_tokens = 32};
cfg.dense_mode = rag::DenseMode::IvfInt8;       // Exact | Int8Rerank | Ivf | IvfInt8
Engine engine(cfg);

engine.ingest("Iceberg orders show a small visible size and refill from a hidden reserve.", /*doc_id=*/1);
engine.ingest("VWAP algorithms follow the intraday volume curve.", 2);
engine.commit();  // freeze BM25 (+ IVF) into a new snapshot; safe while queries run

auto ctx = engine.make_context();  // one per query thread; reuse it
const auto r = engine.query("how do iceberg orders work?", /*k=*/3, ctx);
const rag::ChunkView top = engine.chunk(r.hits[0].id);   // text, doc_id, ordinal, source, page_first, page_last
std::string_view prompt = r.prompt.prompt;               // ready for an LLM
```

`QueryResult` fields:
* `hits`: fused ranking of `Hit{score, id}`, best first;
* `dense`, `lexical`: each retriever's own list;
* `prompt`: a `PromptResult` with `.prompt`, `.chunks_used`,
  `.context_tokens` and `.truncated`;
* `snapshot_version`.

Every span points into `ctx` and stays valid until the next query on that
context.

`EngineConfig` fields, in declaration order (designated initializers must
follow it):

| Field | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `max_chunks` | 65,536 | capacity |
| `chunker` | `{128, 32}` | `window_tokens`, `overlap_tokens` |
| `bm25` | `{1.2, 0.75}` | `k1`, `b` |
| `rrf` | `{60, 1, 1}` | `k0`, `w_dense`, `w_lexical` |
| `prompt` | `{1024, system}` | `max_context_tokens`, system text |
| `dense_mode` | `Exact` | dense retrieval mode |
| `hybrid` | `true` | `false` = dense only |
| `dense_min_score` | `0` | strict cosine floor |
| `retrieve_depth` | 32 | per-retriever list depth fed to RRF |
| `rerank_candidates` | 128 | shortlist size for int8 modes |
| `nprobe` | 8 | IVF lists probed per query |
| `ivf` | `{nlist 0 = auto, train_iters 10, ...}` | IVF parameters |
| `prompt_capacity` | 32 KiB | prompt buffer size |

## 2. Page-aware ingestion and citations

```cpp
const std::string text = "Page one talks about spreads.\n\nPage two talks about maker rebates.";
const std::uint32_t page_starts[] = {0, static_cast<std::uint32_t>(text.find("Page two"))};
engine.ingest(text, 7, rag::IngestOptions{.source = "fees.pdf", .page_starts = page_starts});
// prompts cite "[n] (fees.pdf, p. 2)" or "(fees.pdf, pp. 1-2)" for chunks spanning a page break
```

For real PDFs (Poppler; link `rag_pdf_support`):

```cpp
#include "rag/pdf.hpp"
auto res = rag::pdf::ingest_pdf(engine, "report.pdf", /*doc_id=*/7);  // res.pages, res.chunks, res.empty_pages
```

CLI: `rag_pdf index out.ragidx docs/ a.pdf`, then
`rag_pdf ask out.ragidx "question" -k 5 --prompt`. It also indexes text and
source files verbatim (`.md .txt .hpp .cpp .py ...`, cited by chunk), so
`rag_pdf index project.ragidx .` makes this repository searchable.

## 3. Save, inspect, load

```cpp
engine.save(path);                                   // crash-safe: tmp file, fsync, rename
const rag::IndexInfo info = Engine::inspect(path);   // header only: version, dim, chunks, text_bytes
Engine::LoadReport report;
auto loaded = Engine::load(path, cfg, {}, &report);  // unique_ptr; throws io::FormatError / invalid_argument
// report.reused_indexes: true if the saved BM25/IVF matched cfg and were reused
```

## 4. Batched queries

```cpp
std::vector<std::string_view> queries = {"alpha", "zeta", "gamma delta"};
std::vector<Engine::QueryContext> ctxs;              // one context per query in flight
for (std::size_t i = 0; i < queries.size(); ++i) ctxs.push_back(engine.make_context());
std::vector<Engine::QueryResult> out(queries.size());
engine.query_batch(queries, /*k=*/2, ctxs, out);     // bit-identical to query(); one memory pass per 8
```

## 5. Text cleanup (no Poppler needed)

```cpp
#include "rag/text_clean.hpp"
const auto cleaned = rag::text::clean_pages(pages);  // ligatures, headers/footers, hyphenation, reflow
const auto joined = rag::text::join_pages(cleaned);  // joined.text + joined.page_starts
```

## 6. Train an agent

```cpp
#include "rag/agent/execution_dp.hpp"
#include "rag/agent/trainer.hpp"
using namespace rag::agent;

ExecutionEnv env{ExecutionParams{}};
AgentConfig<3> cfg;                 // 3 = ExecutionEnv::kStateDims
cfg.eps_decay_episodes = 2000;
Agent<ExecutionEnv> agent(env, cfg);
Trainer<ExecutionEnv> trainer(agent, env, {.eval_every = 500, .eval_episodes = 200});
const TrainReport report = trainer.run(4000);   // report.evals, .drift_alarms, .drift_rejected, .bank_recalls, .returns
const Action a = trainer.deployed_act(ExecutionObs{.t = 5, .inventory = 60, .signal = -0.1});

// Measure against the exact optimum on unseen scenarios, paired vs TWAP:
const ExecutionDP dp{ExecutionParams{}};
auto twap = [](const ExecutionObs&) { return ExecutionEnv::kTwapAction; };
EvalStats mine = compare(env, [&](const ExecutionObs& o) { return trainer.deployed_act(o); }, twap, 500, 1);
EvalStats best = compare(env, [&](const ExecutionObs& o) { return dp.act(o); }, twap, 500, 1);
// share of the optimal gain = mine.mean / best.mean
```

`AgentConfig` fields, in order:

| Field | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `learner` | `TileQ` | or `Episodic`, `ActorCritic`, `NeuralQ` |
| `eps_start`, `eps_end`, `eps_decay_episodes` | 1.0, 0.02, 1500 | exploration schedule |
| `bins` | 0 = 8 per dimension | tile grid |
| `tilings` | 8 | number of offset grids |
| `alpha`, `alpha_min`, `alpha_decay_episodes` | 0.1, 0.003, 3000 | annealed step size |
| `gamma` | 1.0 | discount |
| `init_q` | 0 | initial Q-values |
| `double_q` | false | double Q-learning (TileQ); Double DQN (NeuralQ: deployed 87–94% vs 81–87% at 20k, E20) |
| `advantage_learning` | 0 | TileQ and NeuralQ: subtract k × the action's advantage deficit from the target (gap-increasing; 0.5 helped, E19, E20) |
| `replay_capacity`, `replay_per_step` | 100,000, 4 | experience replay |
| `n_step` | 3 | n-step returns (1 = Q-learning, ≥ horizon = Monte Carlo) |
| `actor_alpha`, `critic_alpha`, `temperature` | 0.03, 0.1, 1.0 | `ActorCritic` step sizes and softmax temperature |
| `ac_decay_episodes` | 10,000 | `ActorCritic` annealing half-life (3,000 is too fast, E5) |
| `nn_hidden`, `nn_lr`, `nn_batch` | 64, 1e-3, 32 | `NeuralQ`: units per hidden layer (two layers), Adam step, minibatch |
| `nn_target_sync`, `nn_warmup` | 2,000, 2,000 | `NeuralQ`: updates between target-network copies (0 = none), transitions before learning starts |
| `nn_polyak` | 0 | `NeuralQ`: > 0 deploys an exponential moving average of the weights (avg += τ (net − avg) per update); training is unchanged. τ = 1e-4 beat no averaging on all 8 seeds (94.1% vs 91.5% at 40k, E24) |
| `ac_reexplore_temperature` | 0 | `ActorCritic` re-exploration: 0 = ε-uniform actions; > 0 = sample the policy at a raised temperature (task-dependent, E13) |
| `memory` | | `EpisodicParams` |
| `kernel_width` | 0 = 10% of each range | episodic similarity scale |
| `seed` | 1 | RNG seed |

`TrainConfig` fields, in order:

| Field | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `eval_every`, `eval_episodes`, `eval_seed` | 250, 300, 9,000,000 | held-out evaluation |
| `train_seed` | 1 | training scenario stream |
| `keep_champion` | true | deploy the best policy seen |
| `detect_drift` | true | drift detection on/off |
| `drift` | | `DriftParams`: lag 50, allowance 0.3, threshold 16, baseline_rate 0.001, warmup 1500, two_sided false |
| `drift_after_episodes` | 0 = after the ε schedule | when drift checks start |
| `reexplore_epsilon`, `reexplore_episodes` | 0.5, 5000 | response to drift |
| `forget_on_drift` | true | clear replay on drift |
| `policy_bank`, `bank_capacity` | true, 8 | bank past champions; recall one that fits a returning regime |
| `recall_epsilon`, `recall_episodes` | 0.05, 500 | light exploration after a recall |

`ExecutionParams` (the market) fields that change what is optimal:

| Field | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `lots`, `horizon` | 100, 20 | inventory to sell, steps to sell it in |
| `impact`, `impact_exponent`, `fixed_cost` | 0.01, 2.0, 0 | cost of selling q lots in a step: impact·q^exponent + fixed (if q > 0) |
| `terminal_impact` | 0.05 | leftover inventory is dumped at terminal_impact·rem² (+ fixed) |
| `risk_aversion` | 0 | λ: reward −= λ·noise_sd²·I² per step (mean-variance; E4) |
| `permanent_impact` | 0 | γ: each lot sold lowers the price for the rest of the episode; held inventory loses γ·q·I' (E7) |
| `alpha`, `signal_rho`, `signal_sd`, `noise_sd` | 1, 0.7, 0.1, 0.05 | price move = alpha·signal + noise; signal is AR(1) |
| `sizes` | {0, 0.5, 1, 2, 3} | action k sells sizes[k] × the TWAP rate |

`ExecutionDP{params}` is exact for every combination above;
`ExecutionDP{params, trade_costs, dump_costs}` takes arbitrary cost tables.
`dp.policy_value(policy)` is the exact expected value of any policy under
that model (backward induction with the policy's action).

## 7. Deploy safely: a baseline guard

```cpp
Trainer<ExecutionEnv> guarded(agent, env, {.eval_every = 500, .eval_episodes = 300});
guarded.set_baseline(twap);              // deploy TWAP until the champion beats it by 2 standard errors
guarded.run(4000);
const bool learned = guarded.learned_policy_deployed();   // false = the baseline is live
const Action b = guarded.deployed_act(ExecutionObs{.t = 0, .inventory = 100, .signal = 0.05});
```

## 8. Model-based agent (learn the market, then plan)

```cpp
#include "rag/agent/model_based.hpp"
ExecutionParams structure;               // only lots, horizon and sizes are read; the rest is learned
ModelBasedExecutionAgent mb(structure, {.cost_model = CostModel::Shrunk, .seed = 1});
Rng rng(42);
train_model_based(mb, env, /*episodes=*/50, rng);   // act, observe, end_episode, replan on a schedule
mb.replan();
const ModelEstimate est = mb.current_estimate();      // impact, terminal_impact, alpha, rho, signal_sd, holding
const Action c = mb.act(ExecutionObs{.t = 5, .inventory = 60, .signal = -0.1}, /*explore=*/false);
```

`ModelBasedExecutionAgent::Config` fields, in order: `explore_episodes` 10,
`eps` 0.01, `explore_lo` 0, `explore_hi` 4, `replan_every` 20, `replan_max_gap` 500, `dp_grid` 61,
`cost_model` (`Quadratic`, `PerSize`, or `Shrunk`), `shrink_steps` 30,
`learn_holding_cost` true, `learn_permanent_impact` true, `forget` 1.0,
`detect_drift` false, `drift` (lag 10, allowance 0.3, threshold 10,
baseline_rate 0.01, warmup 50), `drift_forget` 0, `seed` 1. Use `Shrunk`
when the cost curve's shape is unknown (E1c). In a market that may change,
set `detect_drift = true` (E9); `drift_alarms()` lists when it fired.

## 9. Check a model, and warm-start a learner from it

```cpp
// Is the learned model right? Paired predicted vs realized gain over TWAP.
const ModelCheck check = mb.check_model(env, /*episodes=*/100, /*seed=*/6'000'000);
const bool model_ok = check.z < 4.0;       // a wrong model is optimistic: z >> 4

// Distill the model's plan into a tile-coded learner (states visited in the
// model's own simulator: no real episodes spent), then fine-tune it on real
// rewards with the plan as the guard's baseline.
const ExecutionDP& model = *mb.plan();
AgentConfig<3> warm_cfg;
warm_cfg.eps_start = 0.1;
Agent<ExecutionEnv> warm(env, warm_cfg);
warm.distill(ExecutionEnv(model.params()),
             [&](const ExecutionObs& o, Action a) { return model.q_value(o.t, o.inventory, o.signal, a); },
             /*episodes=*/2000, /*seed=*/7);
Trainer<ExecutionEnv> fine(warm, env, {.eval_every = 500, .eval_episodes = 300, .detect_drift = false});
fine.set_baseline([&](const ExecutionObs& o) { return mb.act(o, false); });
fine.run(2000);                            // deployed: never worse than the plan
```

`evaluate()`, `compare()` and `EvalStats` are in `rag/agent/evaluate.hpp`
(included by `trainer.hpp` and `model_based.hpp`), with
`episode_returns(env, policy, n, seed)` (every episode's return, same
scenarios) and `cvar(returns, alpha)` (mean of the worst `alpha` fraction)
for tails (E14).

## 10. Off-policy evaluation from logs

```cpp
#include "rag/agent/ope.hpp"
// Logged under the behaviour policy, with its propensity b(a | obs) > 0:
std::vector<LoggedEpisode<ExecutionObs>> logs = /* {obs, action, reward, propensity} per step */ {};
auto target = [&](const ExecutionObs& o) { return dp.act(o); };
const auto values = mb.plan()->evaluate_policy(target);   // a fitted model's V/Q for the target
const OpeEstimates e = off_policy_estimates(
    logs, target,
    [&](const ExecutionObs& o, Action a) { return values.q(o.t, o.inventory, o.signal, a); },
    [&](const ExecutionObs& o) { return values.value(o.t, o.inventory, o.signal); },
    /*horizon=*/20);
// e.pdis, e.wpdis, e.dr; trust them only if e.min_ess is large and e.uncovered_steps == 0.
const double direct = values.start_value();               // the model's own estimate (DM)
```

At horizon 20 the importance-sampling estimates are unusable unless the
target is close to the behaviour (E10); rely on the model, and check it.

## 11. Another task: market making

```cpp
#include "rag/agent/market_making.hpp"
const MarketMakingParams mp;                // quote distances, fill model, risk penalty
MarketMakingEnv mm(mp);
AgentConfig<2> mm_cfg;                       // 2 = MarketMakingEnv::kStateDims: (t, inventory)
mm_cfg.learner = LearnerKind::ActorCritic;
mm_cfg.eps_decay_episodes = 5000;
Agent<MarketMakingEnv> maker(mm, mm_cfg);
Trainer<MarketMakingEnv> mm_trainer(maker, mm, {.eval_every = 1000, .eval_episodes = 500, .detect_drift = false});
mm_trainer.run(5000);
const MarketMakingDP mm_dp(mp);              // the exact optimum, for scoring
const Action quote = mm_trainer.deployed_act(MarketMakingObs{.t = 25, .q = 4});
// long 4 units: expect a wide bid and a tight ask (mm.bid_distance(quote) > mm.ask_distance(quote))
```

Any type satisfying the `Environment` concept works the same way.

## 12. Tune and analyse (optional: NLopt + Eigen)

```cpp
#include "rag/agent/tuning.hpp"    // NLopt
#include "rag/agent/analysis.hpp"  // Eigen
const std::vector<tuning::Param> space = {
    {"alpha", 0.001, 1.0, /*log_scale=*/true}, {"bins", 2, 10, false, /*integer=*/true}};
auto score = [&](const std::vector<double>& v) { /* train with v on fixed seeds, return the mean share */ return 0.0; };
const tuning::Result r = tuning::maximize_global(space, {0.1, 8}, score, /*global=*/80, /*local=*/40, /*seed=*/1);
// r.best, r.best_score, r.history (every trial). Validate r.best on held-out seeds:
const Interval ci = bootstrap_mean(held_out_scores, 10000, 1);   // evaluate.hpp, no dependency
const auto sens = analysis::quadratic_sensitivity(rows, scores);  // which parameters matter
const Eigen::VectorXd b = analysis::least_squares(X, y, weights); // rank-revealing weighted LS
```

`examples/tune_agent.cpp` (target `tune_agent`) does all of this for
TileQ on ExecutionEnv. `InnovationControlledEnv` (`variance_reduction.hpp`)
wraps ExecutionEnv with a control variate for the price noise (E17).

## 13. Compile a policy into a lookup table

```cpp
#include "rag/agent/policy_table.hpp"
// Any execution policy: here the trainer's deployed champion (e.g. a NeuralQ network).
const ExecutionPolicyTable table(env.params(), [&](const ExecutionObs& o) { return trainer.deployed_act(o); },
                                 {.signal_bins = 64, .signal_range_sd = 4.0});
const Action fast = table.act(ExecutionObs{.t = 3, .inventory = 80, .signal = 0.05});  // a lookup, no allocation
table.save("policy.tbl");                                    // rag::io format: checksummed, crash-safe
const auto loaded = ExecutionPolicyTable::load("policy.tbl"); // throws io::FormatError on a bad file
const auto voted = ExecutionPolicyTable::median({table, loaded, loaded});  // cell-by-cell vote of same-grid tables
```

The table holds horizon × (lots + 1) × bins one-byte actions (129 KB at the
defaults and 64 bins). Signals beyond the range use the edge bins (E22, E23).

## 14. ES order-flow trading (summary; details in ORDERFLOW.md)

```cpp
#include "rag/orderflow/scalp_env.hpp"   // EsSimulator, FeatureBuilder, ScalpEnv
#include "rag/orderflow/replay_env.hpp"  // TickTape, ReplayScalpEnv
using namespace rag::orderflow;
EsSimulator sim(SimParams{}, /*seed=*/1);        // or efficient_market()
sim.step();                                       // 100 ms: sim.trades(), sim.quote()
const auto id = sim.submit_limit(Side::Buy, sim.bid(), 1);  // FIFO queue position: sim.resting()
ScalpEnv env;                                     // an Environment for rag::agent learners
auto tape = std::make_shared<const TickTape>(TickTape::from_csv("es_ticks.csv", {.time_unit = "ns"}));
ReplayScalpEnv replay(tape);                      // same observations, recorded ticks
```

A new environment only needs to satisfy the `Environment` concept: an
`Observation` type, `kNumActions`, `kStateDims`, `kLo`/`kHi`, and
`reset(rng)`, `step(action, rng)` and `features(obs)`.

## 15. The OF15 learning pipeline (Python; `-DRAG_PYTHON=ON`)

Not compiled by `api_examples_tests.cpp`: the binding is tested by
`python/tests` (ctest `ragrl_pytest`, `ragrl_smoke_*`). C++ side:
`rag/orderflow/seed_ledger.hpp` (block table, process roles) and
`rag/orderflow/vec_scalp_env.hpp` (`VecScalpEnv`, `VecReplayEnv`,
`EpisodeSource`, `run_builtin`); module `_ragof` (`python/ragof_module.cpp`).

```python
import _ragof as R                        # RAGOF_DIR = the directory holding _ragof*.pyd
R.forbid_sealed()                         # trainer process: sealed blocks can never open here
p = R.ScalpParams("discovery_v4", 1050.0) # market, warm-up s; fee/latency/... keyword-only
env = R.VecScalpEnv(p, R.EpisodeSource.train(1), n_envs=8, threads=1)  # run seed 1, auto-reset
obs = env.reset()                         # float64 [8, 16] (ScalpEnv::features)
obs, reward, done = env.step(actions)     # actions int64 [8] in {0 short, 1 flat, 2 long}
val = R.EpisodeSource.block("validation", 0, 200)   # Rng(700M + i); test/confirm only via open_sealed
```

Experiments go through the `ragrl` CLI on a JSON config, never through
raw seeds (`PYTHONPATH=python`, the venv `.venv`):
`python -m ragrl run experiments/configs/of15_ppo_v4.json` (train,
select on validation, recheck seed 1, then one sealed scoring in a fresh
process); `report RUN` re-renders from stored files without opening a
block; `confirm` (950M+) is the orchestrator's only.

## FILE: docs/ai/RAG_ENGINE.md  (7,371 bytes)

# RAG engine internals

The engine is `rag::RagEngine<Embedder E, std::size_t MaxK = 64>` in
`include/rag/engine.hpp`. Read this before changing anything in `include/rag/`.

## Data flow

```
INGEST (one writer thread)
  text ─► Arena copy ─► tokenize (Token{offset,len,hash}) ─► chunk_tokens (window/overlap)
       ─► per chunk: Embedder::embed ─► VectorStore::append (fp32 + int8 + scale)
                     Bm25Builder::add (term hashes, stopwords removed)
                     ChunkMeta {text, len, doc_id, ordinal, page_first, page_last, source}
  commit() ─► new Snapshot{Bm25Index frozen, IvfIndex (if an IVF mode)} ─► atomic exchange ─► EBR retire old

QUERY (any number of threads, each with its own QueryContext; zero heap allocations)
  prepare:  tokenize ─► terms (no stopwords) ─► embed ─► has_signal = |qvec|² > 0
  pin snapshot (EpochDomain::Guard), live = store.size() (acquire)
  dense:    Exact      flat fp32 scan of [0, live)
            Int8Rerank int8 scan -> shortlist (rerank_candidates) -> fp32 rerank
            Ivf        IVF over [0, covered) + exact scan of the delta [covered, live)
            IvfInt8    IVF-SQ8 shortlist -> fp32 rerank + exact delta scan
            (admission floor: score > dense_min_score, default 0)
  lexical:  Bm25Index::search over the snapshot (impact sums into a dense accumulator)
  fuse:     RRF(k0 = 60) over the dense and lexical lists
  prompt:   build_prompt into ctx.prompt_buf with citations "[n] (source, p. X)"
```

## Key data structures

| Structure | Layout | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `VectorStore<D>` | separate aligned arrays: fp32 rows, int8 rows, per-row scale; `size_` on its own cache line | scans stream contiguous memory; readers never false-share with the writer |
| `TopK<Cap>` | `std::array` heap + cached threshold + `sorted_` flag | reject path is one compare; NaN rejected; deterministic ties (score desc, id asc) |
| `Bm25Index` | open-addressing term table (power of two, load ≤ 0.5) + SoA postings (doc u32, weight f32) | query = gather-add of precomputed impacts |
| `IvfIndex<D>` | centroids + CSR `offsets/ids` + contiguous reordered rows (fp32 and/or int8) | probing a list is a sequential read |
| `Arena` | list of blocks that never move | `string_view`s into it stay valid for readers |
| `EpochDomain` | 128 reader slots of 64 B each (active epoch, in_use, depth) | per-reader pinning with no shared writes |

## Concurrency contract (enforced by TSan tests and `rag_stress`)

* `ingest()` and `commit()` run on **one writer thread at a time**.
* `query()` / `query_batch()` run on any number of threads, **concurrently
  with ingest and commit**. Each thread owns a `QueryContext`; at most 128
  can be live.
* **Publication:** the writer fills a row and its `ChunkMeta`, then does
  `size_.store(n+1, release)`. Readers do `size()` (acquire) and see only
  complete rows.
* **Snapshots:** `commit()` builds a new `Snapshot` off to the side, publishes
  it with `snap_.exchange(seq_cst)`, retires the old one with the current
  epoch, and frees it once no reader slot holds an older epoch
  (`EpochDomain::quiescent_since`).
* **Lifetime:** destroy every `QueryContext` before the engine.

## Validation added by the property-test review (R13–R17)

* `EngineConfig`: BM25 `k1` must be finite and ≥ 0, `b` in [0, 1].
* `Bm25Index::load()`: every posting weight must be finite and > 0.
* IVF list rows are scored with `simd::dot1` (bit-identical to the exact scan).
* IVF training uses at least one point per list; `build(store, 0)` resets.
* `tests/rag_property_tests.cpp` checks these and the properties above on
  random inputs (48 random corpora × configs × all dense modes against the
  reference implementation).

## Invariants that tests rely on (don't break these)

1. **Bit-exact batching:** `query_batch` returns bit-identical results to
   `query`. This holds because `simd::dot1` matches one lane of `simd::dot4`
   exactly, and the exact scan uses `dot1` for tail rows. Use `dot1`, not
   `dot`, wherever a score must match a `dot4` lane.
2. **Zero allocation:** the query path makes no heap allocations. A global
   `operator new` counter in `rag_tests.cpp` enforces it.
3. **Scratch reset:** `Bm25Scratch::acc` is all zeros between queries.
4. **Ordering:** `TopK` orders by score descending, then id ascending.
5. **Validate before mutating:** `ingest()` checks `IngestOptions` before
   touching anything. Invalid page offsets throw and leave the engine
   unchanged.
6. **Safe loading:** loading never trusts a field. Every offset, length and
   id is checked against the structure it indexes, and `tests/forgery_tests.cpp`
   covers each check with a valid-checksum forged file. Add a forgery case
   for every new field.

## Index file format (version 2, little-endian)

```
FileHeader   40 B  magic "RAGCPPIX", version=2, dim, embedder_tag, n_chunks, reserved, text_bytes
ChunkRecord  40 B × n   text_offset u64, len, doc_id, ordinal, page_first, page_last, source idx (0xFFFFFFFF = none), reserved u64
text blob    text_bytes
sources      vec<u64> offsets (n_sources + 1, from 0), vec<char> names
rows         n × D × f32
Bm25Builder  vec<Triple{term u64, doc u32, tf u32}>, vec<u32> doc_len, u32 num_docs
IndexRecord  40 B  k1, b, nlist, train_iters, max_train, storage, has_ivf, seed, covered
Bm25Index    vec<Slot{key u64, offset u32, len u32}>, u64 nterms, u32 ndocs, vec<u32> doc, vec<f32> weight
IvfIndex     u32 nlist, u32 covered, u8 flags(1 = fp32, 2 = int8), centroids, vec offsets, vec ids, rows...
checksum     u64 (8-byte-word streaming hash over everything before it)
```

* **Reuse on load:** `load()` reuses the saved snapshot if the BM25 and IVF
  parameters match the new `EngineConfig` and it covers every chunk;
  otherwise it rebuilds. `LoadReport::reused_indexes` says which happened.
* **Saving:** writes `<path>.tmp`, fsyncs it, renames it over `<path>`, then
  fsyncs the directory.
* **Format changes:** bump `io::kFormatVersion` for any layout change, and
  extend `tests/forgery_tests.cpp` (its `Layout` walker parses the file).

## Measured performance (Xeon 2.8 GHz VM, AVX-512 VNNI, D = 384, one core)

| Operation | Result |
|---|---|
| fp32 / int8 dot product (384 dims) | 16–32 ns / 7.7–9.6 ns |
| flat fp32 scan, 100k rows | 15.4 ms (~10 GB/s, DRAM-bound) |
| int8 scan + rerank, 100k rows | 2.95–3.6 ms |
| IVF-256 nprobe 16, 100k rows: fp32 lists / int8 lists | 1.1–1.3 ms / 0.32–0.38 ms |
| BM25 query, 5M postings | 7–9 µs |
| end-to-end query, 60k chunks (p50 / p99) | exact 10.1 / 15.2 ms; int8 2.7 / 4.2 ms; ivf 0.62 / 1.08 ms; ivf-sq8 0.28 / 0.43 ms |
| batch of 8 queries | exact 84 → 460 queries/s; int8 428 → 890 queries/s |
| PDF indexing | 400 pages in 0.42 s (≈ `pdftotext` alone) |

Results vary ±15% between runs on this shared VM, and up to 2× for
nanosecond-scale kernels.

## Extension points

* **Embedder:** implement the `Embedder` concept (`kDim`, `kName`, `embed()`
  noexcept, no allocation) to plug in a neural encoder. `kName` goes into
  index files; change it whenever the embedder's output changes.
* **Generator:** anything with `generate(std::string_view)` satisfies the
  concept; see `RagEngine::answer()`.
* **New dense mode:** add it to `DenseMode`, `dense_one()`, `dense_batch()`
  and `uses_ivf()`/`effective_ivf()` if it builds an index, plus the engine
  test parameter lists.
