  by construction), a once-per-run check keyed on the run name (bypassed
  by renaming), test tapes loadable after any dev opening, and a crash
  after opening burning the only opening. All fixed (A78); the plan's
  window count went from ~301 to 292.
* **Dev days** (diagnostics, not a score): 2026-09-14..17 (ESU26, the
  expiring back month after the roll: thinner, 1-tick spread 86.8% of
  buckets vs 94.7% front month, 1-min mid SD 7.2 vs 9.3 ticks) and the one
  usable window of 2026-11-03 (its book has a 610 s recording outage);
  149 windows.
  * **Covariate shift** vs v4 (viz 960,016,000,000+[0,200)): only
    `imb_l5` (9.3% of real values outside v4's 1-99% range) and `absorb`
    (6.2%; v4's range is exactly 0) exceed 5%. Setting them to the sim
    median changes any ensemble's action on <= 0.8% of decisions.
  * **Baselines** (ticks/window): flat 0 exactly; hold −2.5 ± 2.4; delta30
    −4.1 ± 2.2 (v4: −5.12); the imbalance rule (enter with the L1
    imbalance, flat at the next decision) −381.7 at 586 orders/window.
  * **I1 markout** in the imbalance's direction: real +0.022 / +0.072 /
    +0.090 ticks at 100 ms / 1 s / 5 s (strongest quintile +0.126 at 1 s),
    v4 +0.023 / +0.069 / +0.069, against a 1.37-tick real round trip
    (half-spread 0.57 + fees). v4 reproduces the real signal closely; on
    real ES it is about a tenth of the cost of trading it.
  * PPO ensembles (v3 and v4) enter on 100% of flat-state real decisions;
    the other ensembles on 0-0.4%.
* **Next:** phase 2 (score the 16 OF15 runs once on the 8 test days)
  needs the user's go. Expectation from the dev diagnostics: no edge (the
  one signal the agents use is a tenth of the round-trip cost on real ES).
  (Done: OF16 phase 2 below.)

## OF16 phase 2. The 16 OF15 runs on the real ES test days: no edge

`kit.ps1 run of16_phase2 'bash experiments/of16_phase2.sh test'`
(2026-11-12): exported the 8 test days (the orchestrator's flag), then ran
`ragrl score-real RUN --days test --stress` once per sealed OF15 run, each
in a fresh process, single-threaded, ~23 min in all. The same script with
`dev` ran first as a rehearsal on the dev days (all 16 runs, no failures).
Results: `experiments/of16_real_results.txt`, regenerated by
`experiments/of16_summary.py --days test` from `RUN/real/test-*/results.json`
(the opening itself cannot be repeated); a page at
`build-avx512/real/of16_viz/index.html` (`of16-real` in `.claude/launch.json`).

* 292 windows: 2026-09-08..11 (ESU26) and 11-05, 11-09, 11-10, 11-11
  (ESZ26), none excluded for book gaps. Warm-up 1050 s, 600 s windows,
  100 ms latency, $1.50 per side; the stress block (300 ms, $3 per side)
  in the same opening.
* **No headline meets the edge rule** (32 headlines, t_req 4.63). The best,
  lgbm_v4's ensemble, nets +0.40 ± 0.32 ticks/window, t 1.2 (day-clustered
  CI [+0.09, +0.80], Sep +0.24, Nov +0.56), from 6 round trips in 292
  windows, two of them +65 and +55 ticks: noise. The stress block has the
  same top (t 1.2). **Decision (plan §4): no edge on real ES.** The
  simulator-trained RL line is closed; phase 2b does not run.
* **Baselines** (ticks/window): flat 0; delta30 −2.71 ± 1.93 (Sep −3.82,
  Nov −1.57); the L1-imbalance rule −362.0 at 593.5 orders/window, gross
  −290.8, i.e. −0.49 ticks per order before fees: the +0.07-tick signal
  never pays the spread.
* **Transfer:** the agents behave on real ES as they did in their
  simulator. Across the 32 headlines, orders/window in sim and on real
  correlate 0.994 and net 0.88. The 9 headlines that trade more than 0.5
  orders/window lose −1.60 ticks/window on real against −1.24 in their
  simulator. Ensembles are flat or nearly (0-0.2 orders/window; ppo_v3/v4
  ~2); the mean of seeds loses wherever the seeds trade (neuralq_ref_v4
  −4.44, t −11.2; ppo_fs_v4 −2.37; ppo_fs_v3 −1.71).
* **Red flags:** 6 hard RG (3 policies × base and stress) and 2 R1v
  warnings (ppo_v3 seed10: Sharpe 7.8 as a point estimate, lower bound −3.9).
  The RG flags are not leaks. neuralq_ref_v3 seed11's real gross is +0.08
  (t 0.3) against −0.55 in v3; neuralq_ref_v4's ensemble +0.07 (t 1.2, 14
  of 292 windows traded) against −0.27; rppo_v3's mean of seeds +0.001 (one
  trade) against −0.003. Each fires because its simulator gross was
  negative and the real one is about 0. None has real gross above 0 by 2 SE,
  and 3 of the 224 learned policies at a one-sided 2 SE is chance level
  (~5 expected). The phase 1 no-lookahead test holds, and the imbalance
  rule pays its full spread, so fills do not see the decision bucket. The
  status files keep RED_FLAGS (not edited after the fact); the rule's flaw
  is LESSONS 56. A80 then tightened RG for later openings (hard only when
  the real gross is also > 0 by 2 SE on >= 2 orders/window); re-applied to
  these results it gives 6 RGv warnings and no hard flag.
* **Ledger:** 16 `real` test openings, one per OF15 config sha256
  (`build-avx512/rl/ledger_usage.jsonl`). The 8 test days have now been
  seen: a later real-data claim is scored on the confirm block (days from
  2026-11-12), never on them (A79).
* **Next:** plan §4 leaves phase 3 (training on real, walk-forward by day)
  or simulator realism. Phase 1's markout argues against phase 3 for
  directional 1 s market-order policies: the one signal is a tenth of the
  round trip. A different action space (passive orders, longer holds)
  would be a new question with its own pre-registration.

## OF17. A signal census of the agents' features on real ES: nothing to trade

Plan pre-registered in `OF17_SIGNAL_CENSUS_PLAN.md` (commit dea264c, before
any census number). `kit.ps1 run of17_census '... experiments/of17_signal_census.py
--out experiments/of17_signal_census.txt'` (2026-11-12, ~2 min, one thread;
a rerun by the reviewer reproduced the file byte for byte). Data: the 8
front-month days OF16 phase 2 scored, now manifest role `seen` (A81),
opened as `real_seen`; 175,200 samples, OF16's decisions exactly (1050 s
warm-up per 600 s window, fill one bucket after the decision; the imb_l1
alignment check against the tape is exact, 0). Each sample is a 1-lot
market-order round trip held 1, 5, 15, 30, 60, 120, 300 or 600 s at the
recorded touch plus $1.50 per side; rules are fitted on one month (Sep or
Nov) and scored on the other; 128 tests (13 single features × 8 horizons,
LightGBM on all 13 × 8 horizons × 3 prediction cut-offs), t_req 5.12,
day-clustered SE.

* **No candidate.** The highest t of a net is relvol30 at 600 s, +0.23
  ticks per trade, t 0.33. No red flag (t > 10, or > 2 ticks gross at
  h <= 5 s).
* **1-60 s: the signal is a fraction of the cost.** The mean round trip
  costs 1.28 ticks. The best gross per trade is +0.09 at 1 s, +0.10 at 5 s
  and +0.11 at 15 s (all imb_l1), +0.22 at 30 s (ret30) and +0.09 at
  60 s, against detectable nets of 0.12-0.79 ticks. The L1 imbalance is
  the one steady short-horizon signal, as OF16 phase 1 found, at a twelfth
  of the cost.
* **120-600 s: none detected.** Decisions 1 s apart overlap: about 730
  independent outcomes per month at 120 s and 143 at 600 s, so a rule
  needs 1.45-2.81 ticks net per trade to clear t_req. The long-horizon
  features (vwap_dist, delta120, ret120) do predict within a month, but
  the sign flips: vwap_dist's top-minus-bottom quintile move at 600 s is
  +10.0 ticks in November (momentum) and −7.3 in September (reversal);
  fitted on one month, they lose on the other (vwap_dist −3.9 gross per
  trade at 600 s). A walk-forward variant (direction from the previous
  day, the reviewer's exploration, not a test) peaked at +1.01 net, t 1.8.
* LightGBM's out-of-fold R² is negative at every horizon (Nov −0.0005 to
  −0.035; Sep −0.011 to −0.68): month-to-month instability plus, at long
  horizons, overfitting (a 500-row leaf holds about one independent 600 s
  outcome). Plain least squares is negative out of fold too.
* **Review** (independent skeptic): timing (features end at bucket f − 1,
  the fill at f's end quote; ret5 agrees with the f − 1 window in 100% of
  samples), costs, folds and statistics confirmed. It corrected the
  conclusion's wording, from "the features predict nothing" to "no rule
  whose direction holds from one month to the other", and pointed out
  the power limit at long horizons. Both are in the results file. Feature
  caveats, faithful to what the agents saw: features restart every
  window, so vwap_dist is a VWAP over the last 1050-1650 s, not the
  session's, and relvol30's running mean starts at 0 (a sawtooth within
  each window). The first 1050 s after 08:30 and the last ~150 s are never
  sampled.
* **Reading.** Market-order trading on these 13 features is closed at 1 s
  to 1 min, the agents' horizon. What is left is a regime question at
  minutes: a momentum-or-reversal state that holds for a month and flips.
  8 days cannot answer it; the user's ~224 days of `.scid` trades (no
  book) can, for the trade-based features (delta, returns, VWAP distance,
  relative volume), at 2-30 min horizons, with a pre-registered regime
  rule and a walk-forward by month.

## OF18. Month-scale momentum / reversal regimes at 5-30 min: no edge

Plan pre-registered in `OF18_REGIME_PLAN.md` (e9485da, revised after a
design review before any number; amended baf2a7b before any statistic:
the span starts 2025-12-15, since 12-12 was still the back month by
volume). Bars: `tools/scid_to_bars.py --start 2025-12-15 --end 2026-11-11`
(`kit.ps1 run of18_bars`): 1-min front-month RTH bars from the `.scid`
files (1-s bundles through August, tick records with bid and ask from
2026-09-08), into `build-avx512/real/bars/` (gitignored); 236 weekdays
with trades, 7 holiday or half sessions dropped (165-180 gap minutes),
228 used days, 0 OHLC violations, nothing read from the confirm block.
Study: `experiments/of18_regimes.py --out experiments/of18_regimes.txt`
(`kit.ps1 run of18_regimes`, ~30 s; the reviewer's rerun was byte
identical). Walk-forward by day over 168 scored days (2026-03-16 ..
2026-11-11): per feature (ret_5/15/30, delta_5/15/30, vwap_dist), horizon
and lookback W (5, 20, 60 days), day D trades the extreme quintiles in the
direction the W days before it showed; 1-lot market orders entered at the
next bar's open, 1.28 ticks per round trip.

* **Primary** (vwap_dist, 10 min, W 20, the OF17 lead): 4,926 trades,
  gross +0.50, net −0.78 ticks per trade (SE 0.43, t −1.83), negative in
  both halves (−1.13 / −0.44), −1.24 without the 13 OF16 days. No edge;
  the study could have detected a net of 0.85.
* **Secondary** (63 rules at 5, 10 and 15 min, t_req 4.88): no candidate.
  Every rule's net is negative; the best is ret_15 / delta_30 at 15 min,
  −0.23 / −0.24 (t −0.3). Upper bounds: no rule nets more than 1.55,
  2.53 and 3.46 ticks per trade at 5, 10 and 15 min. Gross per trade is
  −1.52 to +1.05: nothing near the cost. At 30 min (descriptive) the best
  is delta_30 W5, +0.95 net, t 0.7.
* **Trade-price bias** (47 days with bid and ask): |bias| <= 0.05 ticks
  for every rule; momentum trades −0.022, reversal +0.020 on average (the
  direction the design review predicted, and negligible).
* **Persistence** (descriptive): the correlation between the trailing
  estimate of a feature's top-minus-bottom move and day D's own value is
  within ±0.19 for most rules; vwap_dist W20 shows −0.19 to −0.41. The
  review showed that this is mostly mechanical: day D's own value exists
  only on the ~50 days that reach both trailing tails (they cross the
  VWAP), is negative by construction there (a no-regime random walk with
  the real volatility gives nearly the same), and scales with the day's
  volatility; with volatility clustering that yields the negative values,
  which after scaling by volatility sit within a day-shuffle null (SD
  ~0.15). No evidence of persistence or anti-persistence either way.
* **Review** (independent): code matches the plan with no lookahead; all
  390 bars rebuilt independently from the records on four days (bundle,
  roll, first tick day, CST tick day) with 0 mismatches; the CT clock and
  front-month choice right; bias formula right. It caught the omitted
  momentum / reversal bias split (added) and the persistence wording
  (corrected in the results file).
* **Reading.** With OF17, market-order trading on order-flow and trend
  features in ES is closed from 1 s to 15 min, within the stated power:
  OF17 tight at <= 1 min, OF18's walk-forward regime rules below 1.5-3.5
  ticks per trade not excluded but nothing points to them. What is left
  for this data is a different way of trading (passive orders, which the
  replay cannot fill honestly without queue data) or different
  information.

## OF19. Footprint patterns on true tick data: no edge

The user asked for order-flow trends, exhaustion and absorption. Their
knowledge base (`SierraChart-KB`, outside the repo) had already tested the
bar-total rules (delta divergence, exhaustion fade, effort-vs-result
absorption: all lost, t down to −8.8) and asked for "one clean run" of the
per-price rules on true tick data. OF19 is that run. Plan pre-registered
in `OF19_FOOTPRINT_PLAN.md` (d659252, revised after a design review
before any number). Data: `tools/scid_to_trades.py` (`kit.ps1 run
of19_trades`): every RTH trade of the front month with its aggressor side
and the record's bid and ask, 47 days (2026-09-08 .. 2026-11-11), ~1M
trades a day, volume equal to the OF18 bars, 0 dropped or zero-side
records, 96.8% of trades at the recorded touch. Study:
`experiments/of19_footprint.py --out experiments/of19_footprint.txt`
(`kit.ps1 run of19_footprint`); 10 warm-up days for trailing thresholds,
37 scored; 1-lot market entries and stops at the recorded touch,
sweep-aware, 100 ms latency, $1.50 per side; targets only on a trade one
tick through.

* **No primary edge, no secondary candidate** (5 primaries at the user's
  Sierra study defaults, t_req 3.79; 20 rules, t_req 4.45). Primaries,
  net per trade: stacked imbalance (r 3, k 3) −0.85 (353 trades, t −0.7);
  absorption (30 s, target 4) −2.10 (169, t −6.4); exhaustion (N 1,
  e 5) −2.19 (788, t −3.2); unfinished auction (d 4) −1.36 (6,169, t
  −9.0); trapped traders (stack trigger) −10.49 (8 trades). The only
  positive nets are P1 variants with 22-148 trades and SEs of 3-11 ticks
  (noise; one +105-tick trade checked as real).
* **Does the footprint condition add anything?** Against the same price
  action without it ("shell", block SE): absorption −0.28 to −1.39,
  exhaustion −0.49 ± 0.65, unfinished auction +0.11 ± 0.17, trapped
  traders (delta) −0.08 to −0.75: nothing distinguishable from zero. The
  KB's own benchmark for unfinished auctions: unfinished and finished
  extremes are revisited at indistinguishable rates (d = 4: 78.3% vs
  78.3% within 15 min, 83.8% vs 84.1% within 30 min, ~17,000 extremes).
* **Mechanics:** exhaustion with e = 0 never fires: on all 37 days, none
  of 6,405 new-low and 6,428 new-high 1-min bars has zero same-side
  aggressor volume at the extreme (a new low prints because someone sells
  into the bid). Absorption grosses 0.70 ± 0.34 below a random-time
  control: about 2 SE and uncorrected, not a finding.
* **Review** (independent): every pattern matches the plan with no
  lookahead; an independent per-record simulator matched 198 trades
  exactly; thresholds use only the 10 days before. It found that Sierra
  stamps the records of one exchange message 1 µs apart, so the first run
  grouped no sweeps; grouping 1-µs chains (the plan's intent) cost every
  rule and control 0.06-0.18 ticks per trade, so it could only make the
  result worse, and the run was redone with it. It also added the shell
  SEs, the short-hold red flag and the zero-side count, and corrected the
  wording ("indistinguishable", not "identical"; the absorption vs random
  difference is not a finding; "vs ctrl" for wide-stop rules measures the
  day's trend).
* **Reading.** With OF17 and OF18 and the KB's bar-level tests: in ES,
  2025-12 .. 2026-11, no order-flow pattern traded with market orders
  (features, regimes, delta divergence, exhaustion, absorption, stacked
  imbalances, unfinished auctions, trapped traders) pays its costs, within
  the stated power. What these tests do not cover: discretionary levels
  ("at a key level"), passive execution, and the book (depth recorded on
  ~13 days only).

## OF20. Absorption and exhaustion at key levels: no edge

Practitioners say these setups work "at a key level". Plan pre-registered
in `OF20_LEVELS_PLAN.md` (0898c06, revised after a design review before
any outcome; only signal counts at levels were looked at). Levels
(`experiments/of20_levels.py`): prior RTH high, low, close, volume POC and
70% value area; overnight high and low; initial balance (from 09:30 CT);
RTH VWAP and ± 1 and 2 SD (from 09:30 CT, rounded), each judged at the
setup's formation time. Study: `experiments/of20_atlevels.py --out
experiments/of20_atlevels.txt` (`kit.ps1 run of20_atlevels`, ~30 s; OF19's
data, signals, fills and statistics; OF19's signal functions gained an
optional `meta` list for the formation time and price, and OF19's results
file reproduced byte for byte after it). Setups: absorption = OF19 P2
(W 15, target 4), exhaustion = OF19 P3 (N 1, e 5); the shell = the same
setup without its footprint condition at the same levels.

* **Primary** (both setups within 2 ticks of any level, 222 trades): net
  +0.10 ticks per trade, t 0.08, halves −1.93 / +2.98; setup − shell
  +1.34 ± 1.21 (mix-weighted). No edge; the bound is 2.47 ticks per trade.
* **Secondary** (8 tests, t_req 4.04): none passes; their bounds are 1.9 to
  21 ticks (25-63 trades each).
* **Absorption** loses everywhere: −1.71 away from all levels (471
  trades, t −6.8), −1.44 at any level; at the VWAP bands −2.12 (59 trades)
  is only slightly worse than away, and its setup − shell −0.96 ± 0.45 is
  ~2 SE picked from 8 tests: not VWAP-specific.
* **Exhaustion** at any level nets +1.44 per trade (119 trades) against
  −2.83 away (669), but that mean rests on a few trades: median −9.24,
  SD 26.7, 34% winners; without its top 3 trades −0.96, without its best
  2 days −0.68. The interaction (setup − shell at levels minus away) is
  +3.64, about 1.6 joint-day SE: descriptive, not a finding. Exhaustion at
  the VWAP bands (+6.02, 62 trades) comes from 27 shorts at +13.54, three
  days contributing ~100 ticks each.
* **Review** (independent): all ten static levels recomputed on four days
  (incl. a Monday and the day after the DST change) matched exactly; no
  lookahead in 55,077 setup and shell trades (formation before signal,
  initial balance and VWAP matches only from 09:30 CT); four trades
  re-simulated from raw ticks matched; the rerun is byte identical. It
  corrected the wording of the exhaustion and absorption-at-VWAP lines
  (above) and recomputed the interaction SE with a joint resample (2.26;
  the results file's hypot gives 2.32).
* **Reading.** At the standard auction-market levels, absorption and
  exhaustion do not make money after costs on these 37 days, and the
  footprint condition does not beat a plain touch of the level. Within
  the power (2.5 ticks per trade for the primary), the "at a key level"
  claim is not supported. The only loose thread is exhaustion at levels,
  carried by a handful of trades; it would need many more days (tick
  records accumulate ~20 a month) and its own pre-registration.

## OF21. Forward test of exhaustion at key levels: pre-registered, not run

OF20's one positive descriptive row (exhaustion, OF19 P3 N 1 e 5, within 2
ticks of any OF20 level: +1.44 per trade on 119 trades, −0.96 without its
top 3) gets a forward test on days recorded from 2026-11-12 on, at the
user's request. Plan `OF21_FORWARD_PLAN.md`, revised after a design review
before any forward day was read; runner `experiments/of21_forward.py`,
frozen with OF19/OF20's code by sha256 (plan section 8, checked by
`python/tests/test_of21.py` and before every look).

* **Design:** looks at 60, 125 and 250 full front-month RTH days
  (Lan-DeMets O'Brien-Fleming, one-sided α 1 − Φ(2); boundaries t ≥ 4.504,
  3.022, 2.007), non-binding futility at net ≤ 0, deadline 2028-03-31.
  An edge needs net > 0, t ≥ the boundary, setup − shell > 0 and stress
  net > 0.
* **Power (the red flag):** the in-sample SE is about 13.2 / sqrt(days);
  the final look has power 0.39 if the true edge is +1.44 and 0.94 at +3.
  Confirming +1.44 at 80% power would take about 680 days.
* **Guards:** ledger blocks `real_forward` (one per look; cumulative, in
  order, never repeated except a crash rerun with the same fingerprint)
  and `real_forward_scan` (dates, front month, full or not and a records
  hash; no prices). A day's status is fixed by its first scan; a full day
  whose records later change refuses every later look. The converters
  still refuse days from 2026-11-12; OF20's `overnight()` reads one only
  for a day opened as a forward look. Roll windows need the next contract
  recorded tick by tick (ESH27 by 2026-12-04).
* **Rehearsal** (`experiments/of21_rehearsal.txt`, `kit.ps1 run
  of21_rehearsal`): the runner, reading the .scid files itself, reproduces
  OF20's row exactly on its 37 days (119 trades, +1.44, SE 2.17, shell
  925, setup − shell +2.61 ± 2.23); all 37 pass the full-day rule. OF20's
  results file still reproduces byte for byte after the `overnight()`
  guard change.

## FILE: docs/ai/DECISIONS.md  (37,846 bytes)

# Decision log

Each entry gives the decision, the alternatives considered, and the evidence
behind it. Bugs that shaped a design are included, because they explain why
the code looks the way it does.

## RAG engine

| # | Decision | Why (evidence) |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Hybrid retrieval: dense + BM25, fused by RRF (k0 = 60) | Rank fusion needs no score calibration between cosine and BM25. Exact identifiers (e.g. "ZX81Q") are found by BM25 even when embeddings blur them. |
| R2 | Zero allocation on the query path, with per-thread `QueryContext` scratch | Allocation is a source of latency spikes; enforced by a test counter. |
| R3 | `dense_min_score = 0` (strict) | **Bug found by coverage review:** stopword-only chunks have all-zero embeddings, scored 0, and still entered prompts. |
| R4 | int8 rows with fp32 rerank of 128 | Scans are DRAM-bound (~10 GB/s); int8 moves 4× fewer bytes: 15.4 ms → 2.95 ms on 100k rows, recall ≥ 0.95 in tests. |
| R5 | IVF-SQ8 (int8 inverted lists + fp32 rerank) | p99 1.08 → 0.43 ms at 60k chunks against fp32 IVF. |
| R6 | Batched queries, 8 per memory pass | Exact 84 → 460 queries/s. Bit-exactness needed `simd::dot1` (one `dot4` lane), because `dot()`'s 4-accumulator summation order differs. |
| R7 | `commit()` concurrent with queries via immutable snapshots + epoch-based reclamation | Readers pin with one store to their own cache line. A mutant that frees without EBR is caught by `rag_stress` under ASan within seconds. |
| R8 | Storage allocated lazily, query scratch pre-faulted | Huge-page first touch was 11× slower (1.17 s per 200 MB). Writer-filled storage never faults on readers. Load dropped from 5.7 s to < 1 s. |
| R9 | Index file: checksum plus structural validation of every field | **Bug found while testing:** `offset + len > total` overflowed, so a forged file could point a chunk out of bounds; checksums don't stop forgers. |
| R10 | Saves use a tmp file, fsync, rename, then fsync the directory | Rename alone is atomic but not durable after power loss. |
| R11 | Saved snapshot reused on load when the parameters match | IVF training dominated load time (4.2 s). |
| R12 | Arena bounds checked on integer addresses | **Bug found by fuzzing:** `nullptr + n` on a fresh arena. GCC's UBSan misses it, so CI also runs Clang's. |

| R13 | `EngineConfig` validation rejects BM25 `k1 < 0` or non-finite, and `b` outside [0, 1] | **Bug found by the RAG review agent:** `k1 = -1` made every posting weight 0 and the next query overflowed a heap buffer in `Bm25Index::search`. |
| R14 | `Bm25Index::load()` rejects posting weights that aren't finite and > 0 | **Bug:** a forged file with a valid checksum and zero weights loaded, then overflowed at query time (checksums don't stop forgers; R9). |
| R15 | IVF scores every list row with `simd::dot1` | **Bug:** the tail of each list used `dot`, so a row's score depended on its position: 46 of 1,280 hits differed from the exact scan with every list probed. Same root cause as R6. |
| R16 | IVF training samples at least one point per list; `build(store, 0)` resets the whole index | **Bugs:** `max_train_points_per_list = 0` divided by zero (SIGFPE) on the first IVF commit; rebuilding a used index over zero rows let `save()` write a file its own `load()` rejects. |
| R17 | `BufWriter::put` skips empty writes | **Bug (UB):** an empty `string_view` with a null data pointer reached `memcpy`; UBSan caught it with an empty system prompt. |

## PDF support

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | Poppler (poppler-cpp), reading-order layout | Multi-column pages come out column by column. Optional dependency. |
| P2 | Normalize ligatures (U+FB00–FB06) | Real fonts make Poppler emit U+FB01, which never matches "fi" in queries. |
| P3 | Running header/footer removal: lines recurring at page edges on ≥ 50% of pages (and both pages of a 2-page document) | A real 2-page handout kept its header under the first rule (minimum 3 pages). |
| P4 | Only lines of ≤ 6 words may differ in their digits and still match | Otherwise "Q3 revenue grew 12% ..." vs "Q4 ... 15% ..." would be deleted as a header. |
| P5 | `join_pages` always separates pages, even empty ones | An empty first page would otherwise share a start offset with page 2. |

## Agent

| # | Decision | Why (evidence) |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | An exact DP optimum as the yardstick; report the share of the optimal gain | "Reward went up" can't be compared; "67% of optimal" can. |
| A2 | Paired evaluation on common random numbers | Episode return sd ≈ 55, much larger than the differences between policies. |
| A3 | Tile coding 8×8×8 with 8 tilings (not finer) | 12³ cells: 40%; one cell per time step: 15% (vs 65% at 30k episodes). Generalization beats resolution. |
| A4 | Step size annealed `alpha/(1+ep/3000)` with floor 0.003 | Reward noise ±2.5 per step vs action gaps < 1; the floor keeps the agent adaptable. |
| A5 | Double Q-learning off by default | Level at 30k episodes (64.8 vs 64.5%), worse at 60k (64% vs 72%). |
| A6 | Champion deployed separately from the learner | **Bug:** restoring the champion into the learner at the end of each `run()` undid learning (recovery stalled at 16%). |
| A7 | Drift detection: CUSUM suspects, champion re-score confirms | A learning dip at episode ~34k looked like drift; acting on it cost 18 points. A champion's held-out score is exactly reproducible in an unchanged world. |
| A8 | CUSUM k = 0.3, h = 16, baseline from samples ≥ 50 episodes old, rate 0.001 | Simulated: ~1 false alarm per 200k episodes; a 0.55σ shift detected after a median of 51 episodes. The first detector's baseline absorbed the shift it was meant to detect. |
| A9 | Re-explore with ε 0.5 over 5,000 episodes, and clear replay on drift | Short boosts (0.3 / 600) recovered 10% vs 43%. Clearing replay helps in the first 2,500 episodes only. |
| A10 | Model-based agent: grey-box regression plus a DP planner | 99–100% of optimal after 20–50 episodes vs 67% after 40,000 model-free (E1a). |
| A11 | Model-based `replan()` forces the assumed form (exponent 2, no fixed cost) | **Bug:** the true exponent and fixed cost leaked from `structure` into the planner. Regression test: identical experience with different hidden parameters must give identical plans. |
| A12 | Cost model choice: Shrunk (empirical Bayes, k = 30) is the safe default under uncertainty about the form | Quadratic: 53% when wrong. Per-size: 100% eventually but −1,400% to −1,800% on 4 of 10 seeds at 10 episodes. Shrunk: never below 82% (E1c). |
| A13 | `n_step = 3` by default | Best of n ∈ {1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20}: faster early (47–50% vs 36–45% at 10k) and consistent at 40k (69–70% vs 62–71%); 75–77% at 200k (E2a). |
| A14 | Don't re-tune the representation to fix the plateau | Fitting the default tiles to the true Q* gives 99.5% (E2b). The gap is target noise and coverage (E2c). |
| A15 | Policy bank on by default: bank champions on confirmed drift, recall one that beats the champion by 2 standard errors | Returning regimes are recalled within 20–131 episodes, at their old level, vs −82% to −109% without the bank (E3). The first visit to a regime is unaffected. |
| A16 | Risk aversion as a per-step reward penalty λ·σ²·I², not as a change to cash | Keeps the cash accounting identity testable, and makes the DP exact for the mean-variance objective. The model-based agent fits the penalty as a regressor on −I² (κ recovered to ~2%, E4). |
| A17 | Model-based estimator: one weighted normal-equations solve over all parameters | Separate fits of alpha and costs are biased once a holding term exists, because inventory correlates with both. Gaussian elimination with partial pivoting and a 1e-9 ridge; ≤ 3 + lots unknowns. |
| A18 | `LearnerKind::ActorCritic` anneals with its own half-life, `ac_decay_episodes = 10000` | The TileQ half-life (3,000) froze the policy near-uniform on 1 of 3 seeds (−235% at 10k). 10,000 gave 75–78% at 10k and 80–86% at 40k (E5). |
| A19 | Optional baseline guard (`Trainer::set_baseline`): deploy the learned champion only after it beats the baseline by 2 standard errors of the paired difference, re-checked at every evaluation and drift | Unguarded TileQ deployed −35% to −106% policies in a weak-signal market (E6). Guarded: TWAP on every seed, identical results in the default market (E6b). Opt-in, because it needs a meaningful baseline. |
| A20 | `learn_permanent_impact = true` by default | Without the term, permanent impact drops the model-based agent to 22–55% and it degrades with more data; with it, 99.8–99.9%. The extra parameter costs ≤ 0.3 points elsewhere (E7b). |
| A21 | Check a learned model by its *paired* predicted vs realized gain over TWAP, one-sided z > 4 | Exact policy evaluation makes the prediction noise-free. Pairing cuts the noise ~5×: 100 episodes separate right (\|z\| ≤ 2.5) from wrong (z ≥ 8.5) models; unpaired needed ~1,500. One-sided because a planner exploiting model errors is always optimistic (E7c). |
| A22 | Warm start by distillation, and always under a guard whose baseline is the model's plan | Fine-tuning alone wrecks a correct model's 99% policy (35–57% for α 0.1–0.01: TD noise floor ≈ √(α/2)·σ > action gaps). With the guard: 100% when the model is right, ≥ the plan and 78–82% when it's wrong (E7d). |
| A23 | `evaluate()`/`compare()` live in `evaluate.hpp`, not `trainer.hpp` | The model-based agent needs paired evaluation for `check_model()` but shouldn't depend on the trainer and the model-free stack. `trainer.hpp` includes it, so no caller changes. |
| A24 | Library default keeps `eps_start = 1`; for online learning on ExecutionEnv-like tasks start at 0.1 | Online, ε 1 lost 1,124–1,504 optimal-episodes by episode 5,000 and broke even at ~12k; ε 0.1 broke even at 1.5k–2.1k and learned as well at 10k/40k/100k (E8). Sparse-reward tasks (the corridor) have no optimistic initial values, so the generic default stays exploratory. |
| A25 | Model-based adaptation by change detection + reset, not constant forgetting; both opt-in | Detection recovered to 95–100% within 20 episodes of a flip with 3 false alarms in 27,000 stable episodes and no stable-market cost; forgetting 0.95 recovered as fast but fell to 54–93% in a noisy stable market (E9). Off by default so stationary results and tests are unchanged. |
| A26 | `DriftDetector` lives in `drift.hpp` | Shared by the trainer and the model-based agent without the agent depending on the trainer (same reasoning as A23). |
| A27 | Off-policy estimators ship with diagnostics (`min_ess`, `uncovered_steps`), and the docs recommend model-based (DM) evaluation plus a model check over IS at this horizon | At horizon 20, IS/WPDIS/DR errors were 45–2,968% of the optimal gain even with 10,000 logs (ESS ≈ 0), against ≤ 4% for DM with the right model. DM's bias under a wrong model doesn't shrink with data (−62% at 1k and 10k), so the model must be checked (E10). |
| A28 | DP solve uses a precomputed banded expectation operator; `act()`/`q_value()` keep the direct quadrature | On the grid, E[V(t+1, rem, g') \| g_j] is one linear map for all t, inventories and actions. Solve 63 → 7.1 ms (61-point grid) and 95 → 19.7 ms (121); max \|ΔV\| 6e-13 and no action changes on 360,000 random states vs the old code. The model check reuses the DP optimum for the plan's value: 47.8 → 3.6 ms. |
| A29 | Model-based `eps` default 0.01 (was 0.05); the first plan is made right after `explore_episodes` | ε 0.05 cost 6–10% of the online gain over 1,000 episodes with no gain in final quality (E11). **Bug:** the first plan waited for `replan_every` too, so the agent explored for 20 episodes, not 10; regression test `ModelBased.FirstPlanComesRightAfterExploration`. |
| A30 | Second task (market making) as its own header with an exact DP, using the generic agent unchanged | Shows the `Environment` concept carries the whole architecture; the contrast (97–100% here vs ~70% on execution) isolated the cause of the execution plateau: gain/return-sd 4.07 vs 0.27 (E12). |
| A31 | Detect change at every evaluation from the champion's re-score (any direction); an alarm no longer resets the detector (`reset()` on confirmed change, `rebase()` on a false one); two-sided CUSUM available | An improved market went unnoticed for 1,000+ episodes: CUSUM looked only for drops, and a rejected alarm had left it blind for 1,550 episodes (bug). The re-score is exactly reproducible in an unchanged world, so it's a zero-false-alarm detector with latency ≤ `eval_every` (E13). |
| A32 | `ac_reexplore_temperature` is opt-in | Temperature-based re-exploration fixed an actor-critic stall on market making (88–92% → 95–99%) but was worse on execution's harmful-policy flip (29–30% vs 37–52%) (E13). |
| A33 | Report tails (CVaR on cash outcomes), not just means; `episode_returns()`/`cvar()` in `evaluate.hpp` | The risk-neutral execution optimum has a higher sd than TWAP but a far better worst 5% (−66 vs −117); a mean-variance penalty halved the sd but barely moved the tail in execution, while in market making it lifted the worst 5% from −5.7 to +13.7 (E14). |
| A34 | The model-based market maker estimates fill probabilities per distance (counts with a Beta(1, 1) prior), not a parametric curve | With three distances a non-parametric model costs nothing and covers any fill curve: 98–100% after 3 episodes on both an exponential and a steep curve (E15). |
| A35 | `LearnerKind::NeuralQ` (from-scratch MLP, DQN-style) is available, not the default; TileQ stays the default | NeuralQ reaches 84–89% at 40k vs 65–69% for TileQ, at ~75× the training cost per episode and ~45× the decision latency (1.7 µs vs 39 ns). The cause is generalization (target network and Adam ablated: no effect / small effect) (E16). |
| A36 | Coarser tiles (e.g. 2×4×4 × 16) are recommended for ExecutionEnv; the library default stays 8 per dimension | 76–77% at 40k vs 65–69%, with a 98.3% representation ceiling vs 99.4%. Other tasks' tests are tuned on the default (E16). |
| A37 | `InnovationControlledEnv` (control variate for the price innovation) is a tool, not a default | It removes I′·ε exactly (values unchanged), but in the default market that is ~5% of the return variance; the signal path dominates (sd 53–56 vs 12–13). Where the innovation is large (noise_sd 0.2) it lifted TileQ from 49–61% to 67–71% (E17). |
| A38 | NLopt and Eigen are optional dependencies (`RAG_OPTLIBS`), used only by `tuning.hpp`, `analysis.hpp`, `tune_agent` and their tests | They bring proper derivative-free optimization and rank-revealing least squares without touching the dependency-free core. CI installs them. |
| A39 | Hyperparameter searches go global first (CRS2), then local (Subplex), and are always validated on held-out seeds with bootstrap intervals | A local search alone found 57% or 70% depending on its initial steps; winners scored 0–18 points higher on their own seeds than on unseen ones (E18). |
| A40 | Model-based agents: the gap doubles only after a re-plan; greedy acting before the first plan returns a fixed baseline action; `observe()` alone separates the last sale from the dump | Bugs found by the adversarial-review agent: the first plan's gap doubled too early (plans at 10, 50, 130 instead of 10, 30, 70), greedy evaluation consumed the agent's RNG, and trainers calling only `observe()` biased `terminal_impact` (0.038 vs 0.050). Each has a regression test (`agent_edge_case_tests.cpp`). |
| A41 | `cvar` counts the worst fraction with a rounding tolerance; `evidence()` is the larger of both CUSUM statistics; empty OPE logs cover no step; `ExecutionDP` rejects grids < 2 and floors a zero-width grid | Bugs found by the same review: 0.29 × 100 = 28.999… dropped an outcome; two-sided evidence read 0 until an upward alarm; empty logs reported full coverage; `signal_sd = 0` made every value NaN and `grid < 2` wrote out of bounds. |
| A42 | `advantage_learning` (k) is available for TileQ, default 0 | k = 0.5 gave +5 points at 40k (70–75%) and ~2× the share at 5k; k = 0.9 widened the spread across seeds (E19). Other tasks' tests are tuned on k = 0. |
| A43 | For `NeuralQ`, `double_q` means Double DQN and `advantage_learning` applies; both stay opt-in | Deployed 87–94% at 20k with either or both vs 81–87% plain, and a far more stable greedy policy (87–91% vs 70%); two seeds, so the defaults are unchanged (E20). |
| A44 | Deploy learned execution policies as `ExecutionPolicyTable`s (≥ 32 signal bins) | Same gain as the network within 0.25 points, ~1,000× faster (3 ns), no model code at decision time; saved in the checksummed `rag::io` format (E22). |
| A45 | Ensemble seeds with the cell-wise median of their tables | 8 seeds: 97.5% vs 91.4% average and 94.1% best single seed; better than every member on fresh scenarios (z ≥ 13); median ≥ plurality at all sizes but one; at equal training compute, 4 × 40k voted (96.7%) beats one 160k network (93.5%) (E23). |
| A46 | `ExecutionPolicyTable` checks its grid: the cell count must not overflow or exceed `vector::max_size()`, the loops use 64-bit counters, and the bin width must be a normal double, in the constructor and in `load()` | Bugs found by the review agent: a huge grid wrapped the size to 0 and wrote past the table; `lots = UINT32_MAX` looped forever; a subnormal width (1e-150 × 1e-160) sent every bin centre to the last bin, and `load()` accepted such files. Regression tests in `policy_table_tests.cpp`; `fuzz_policy_table` in CI found the last one unaided. |
| A47 | `nn_polyak` (weight averaging for NeuralQ) is available, default 0; recommended 1e-4 | Deployed 94.1% vs 91.5% at 40k, better on 8/8 seeds, +6.2 points at 20k; training unchanged, one extra copy of the weights. Opt-in like A43, so other tests keep their behaviour (E24). |
| A48 | Recommended deployment for a learned execution policy: 3–4 NeuralQ networks (`double_q`, `nn_polyak = 1e-4`, different seeds), each compiled to an `ExecutionPolicyTable`, combined with `median()` | 96.6–97.1% of the optimum (8 networks: 97.8%) at 3 ns per decision; weight averaging and voting add up with diminishing returns (E25). |
| A49 | ES order-flow simulator: mechanisms (meta-orders, quote fading, icebergs, activity clustering), not curve fitting; capped fading and mean-zero activity excitation | Uncapped fading and raw-move excitation are supercritical (the book blew up at 6,000 trades/s); mechanisms reproduce stylized facts and make order flow predictive for the reasons real markets do (OF1). |
| A50 | Queue model: cancellations biased to the back (power 3), ~120 faster lots join ahead of a 100 ms order | Uniform cancellations let naive two-sided quoting win every episode (+20.7 ticks / 5 min); with the defaults it is breakeven before fees and loses after, the stylized fact for slow 1-lot participants (OF4). |
| A51 | The same `FeatureBuilder` for simulated and recorded ticks, self-normalized by running RMS | Simulator-trained agents run unchanged on recorded data (`ReplayScalpEnv`); backtest Sharpe on a simulated day replayed from CSV matches the in-simulator value (0.74/0.70 vs 0.76/0.77). |
| A52 | `maker_info` (informed makers) is opt-in; `efficient_market()` preset | Default results stay reproducible; the preset removes 30 s delta predictability (corr -0.02) and flattens the markout curve, the regime real ES is closer to (OF9). |
| A53 | NeuralQ allocates no tile tables | They were unused but (bins+1)^Dims: 3.8 GB per agent with two snapshots at 21 inputs; OOM-killed training (2 MB now). |
| A54 | Tick scalper trained off-policy from rule-guided episodes; deployed greedy or rule-anchored | Plain exploration never gets filled (0.1 fills/episode); guided training quotes; anchored deployment matches the rule, greedy loses (OF7). |
| A55 | Train many seeds in parallel threads (`of_train`, `of_calibrate --seeds`), not on GPUs | The networks are 2 x 64 units: per-update work is microseconds, so cores, not GPUs, are the bottleneck; results don't depend on the thread count. |
| A56 | OF10 v1 (opt-in rigid whole-book quote jumps, `discovery_market_v1()`) is not the final price-discovery design | It fixed the gap (corr with the next 30 s 0.57–0.64 → 0.11, floor 0.09–0.10) but decoupled the price from the book: imbalance → next mid change +0.02 vs +0.33 by default; impact R² only through the fixed sniper clip (0.80 vs 0.15); the gap never exceeds the band, so the oracle bound is vacuous (OF10). |
| A57 | OF10 v2: stale-side depletion (quotes value has passed drain lot by lot, nobody joins them, the price ticks when the stale best is empty) plus permanent impact of flow surprise; chosen over the "efficient-price quoting" design | The judge's reasons: in the alternative, an EWMA of flow surprise telescopes into transient impact, which is the predictable fade the fix must remove; and its rate ramp toward the efficient price is the OF9 rate tilt that leaked value into the book. Depletion kept imbalance informative for the next tick only (+0.25, tail ~0) with the gap at the grid floor (OF10). |

The hashes are recorded with MSVC x64 (library `exp`/`log` and FMA contraction differ between compilers); elsewhere the tests only check that the presets differ. |

A C++ allow-list would not reach the `_ragof` copies in each sealed run's `RUN/bin` that actually score OF15, and swapping them would break provenance. ## FILE: docs/ai/CONVENTIONS.md  (3,641 bytes)

# Conventions and working rules

These are the rules the codebase has followed so far. Following them keeps
every claim in the docs measurable and every change verifiable.

## Code

* **Language:** C++20, header-only, `#pragma once`, namespace `rag` (agent:
  `rag::agent`).
* **Hot paths** (query, `act()`) don't allocate. Use fixed `std::array`
  scratch, `TopK<Cap>` on the stack, and per-thread contexts. Tests enforce
  this with the global `operator new` counter (`begin_alloc_count()` /
  `end_alloc_count()` in `tests/test_util.hpp`).
* **Cold paths** (ingest, commit, load, training setup) may allocate, and
  should allocate once.
* **Warnings:** builds use `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Wconversion` with
  `-Werror` in CI. Silence a narrowing conversion with an explicit
  `static_cast`, not a pragma.
* **Bounds checks** are written so they can't overflow, e.g.
  `len > total || off > total - len`, never `off + len > total`.
* **Untrusted input** (index files, PDFs, query text): validate everything
  before use and before mutating any state. Error messages name the failed
  check; tests match on them.
* **SIMD:** dispatch at compile time (`__AVX512F__`, `__AVX2__`, scalar
  fallback). Any score that must equal a `dot4` lane uses `simd::dot1`.
* **Determinism:** seeded `rag::agent::Rng` everywhere (no `std::mt19937`).
  Greedy evaluation must not consume the agent's RNG.
* **Comments** explain why, and quote measurements where a choice was made
  on evidence.

## Tests

* Every bug fix gets a regression test, and the test is shown to fail on the
  old code (mutation check) before it's trusted.
* Prefer exact checks (accounting identities, bit-exact equality, brute-force
  references) over loose ones.
* Statistical claims use paired comparisons on common random numbers, with
  thresholds set below the range measured across seeds and the measurement
  quoted in a comment.
* **Test files:**
  * `rag_tests.cpp`: engine;
  * `forgery_tests.cpp`: loader checks (add a case for every new file field);
  * `reference_tests.cpp`: spec-level reference;
  * `agent_tests.cpp`: agent;
  * `api_examples_tests.cpp`: the snippets in `docs/ai/API.md`.

## Verifying a change (what "done" means here)

```bash
cmake -S . -B build -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release && cmake --build build -j && ctest --test-dir build -j4
```

1. **Windows only (since 2026-11-04, the user's decision).** The project is
   built and run with MSVC on the user's Windows server. The gate is the
   full build and test suite there (`cmake --preset windows-msvc-avx512`,
   `cmake --build --preset windows-avx512-release --parallel`,
   `ctest --preset windows-avx512-release`) with no MSVC warnings. GitHub CI
   runs only `msvc-windows`; the Linux GCC/Clang/sanitizer/fuzz jobs were
   removed (restore them from `e0a4c7a`'s `ci.yml` if Linux matters again).
   Portable C++ is still the habit, but GCC/Clang are no longer verified.
2. **For loader or parser changes**, fuzz:
   `-DRAG_FUZZ=ON -DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=clang++`, `make_seeds`, then run
   `fuzz_load` / `fuzz_text` for a few minutes.
3. **For performance claims**, use `rag_bench` / `rag_latency`, and update
   `README.md` and `bench_results.txt` from the same run.

## Git

* Work on a branch. Fast-forward `main` to a commit whose full gate passed
  on the Windows server.
* Commit messages explain what changed and why, with measured results.

## Documentation

* Numbers in docs come from a recorded run (benchmark output, demo output,
  test thresholds). Re-measure rather than copy when the code changes.
* Experiments go in `docs/ai/EXPERIMENTS.md`, including the ones that
  failed.

## FILE: docs/ai/STATUS.md  (13,411 bytes)

# Status, limitations and next steps

## Works and is verified

* **ES order-flow trading** (`include/rag/orderflow/`, see `ORDERFLOW.md`):
  a tick-level ES simulator calibrated to stylized facts, order-flow
  features, a scalping environment (RL beats every rule, robust to 9
  stresses, but the simulator is too predictable for the absolute P&L to
  mean anything), a tick-scalping environment with FIFO queue position
  (RL matches but doesn't beat the delta-skew rule), a backtester on
  recorded ticks, calibration tooling, a parallel trainer for many-core
  servers, and an anonymous research crawler. Known flaw, first on the
  plan: price discovery is too slow in the simulator (the hidden value
  gap predicts the next 30 s at ~0.6), so RL profits there are inflated;
  the "efficient" preset leaks value through the book (OF9).
  **Order-flow status (2026-11-05):** OF10 iteration 3 is done
  (`e229af4`): an opt-in stationary activity model fixes the warm-up (the
  legacy activity loop is unstable), and the v3 `discovery` preset passes
  the gap, imbalance (marginally), stylized-fact and naive-rule criteria
  with oracles that trade, on tuning, held-out (13–18, once) and fresh
  skeptic seeds. It fails passive quoting (−24 to −27 ticks/episode, stale
  sweeps), so it is accepted for market-order RL only, and meta-orders
  need a 1,050 s warm-up (A62–A66). The RL reruns (OF11, `1a73bd6`) find no edge on `discovery`:
  every seed negative on sealed episodes, ensemble flat, all stresses and
  trading days ≤ 0. OF12 compared v3 with the user's real ES (Sierra):
  imbalance and price-on-delta match; the book is ~8× too deep and the
  mid lags the flow by a second. OF13 (`31f51c4`) added simulator v4,
  the opt-in `discovery_v4` preset (v3 frozen, golden-pinned): all 25
  pre-registered real-ES gate rows (G1–G8) and the efficiency gates pass
  on tuning, held-out (once) and skeptic seeds at the 1,050 s warm-up
  (A67–A69). Both oracles lose after costs on v4, so "no RL edge" there
  is close to true by construction (LESSONS 48); caveats: I1 still
  predicts the next 1 s, the mid flickers, the spread never widens, and
  the real reference is one calm morning. A live training viewer
  (`of_train_live`, `tools/of_train_live.py`) watches RL runs (A70).
  OF14: RL on v4 finds no edge either
  (all 24 seeds negative, mean −3.18 ticks/episode).
  **OF15 (2026-11-12):** a leak-proof learning pipeline (A71–A73): C++
  `seed_ledger.hpp` (process roles, `forbid_sealed`, privileged channel
  on train/viz only) and `vec_scalp_env.hpp` (`VecScalpEnv`,
  `VecReplayEnv`, `run_builtin`), the nanobind module `_ragof` (CMake
  `RAG_PYTHON`, OFF by default), and `python/ragrl` (runner, paired
  evaluation, bootstrap CIs, red flags, Optuna, live-viewer hook;
  `experiments/of15_regen.sh`). Eight agent families (NeuralQ reference,
  PPO, frame-stack PPO, recurrent PPO, DQN, QR-DQN, LightGBM,
  BC/DAgger) × v4 and v3, 12 seeds each, one sealed opening per config:
  **all 16 NO_EDGE, no hard red flag.** Most ensembles stay flat (5/8 v4,
  4/8 v3 place 0 orders), so the mean of seeds is the informative number:
  ≤ 0 everywhere on fresh episodes. PPO v4 holds one ~597 s position (a
  coin flip); I1-timing agents gain +0.1 … +0.3 ticks gross at 1 s but pay
  ~0.55 ticks slippage. Open: the 0xc0000374 heap crash (not root-caused;
  0 in ~1,450 evaluations since, sealed results judged unaffected), the
  post-hoc R1v flag change (A74), and WMI broken on this server since
  ~2026-11-07 (bypassed in `ragrl`, A76; repair is the user's call).
  Training is simulator-bound for PPO and update-bound for the DQN family
  on small MLPs: no GPU now (A75). **Caveat:** the legacy
  activity model is unchanged, so every OF2–OF9 result still ran at ~4
  trades/s instead of 15–22 (ORDERFLOW.md §5).

* **Platforms:** Windows only since 2026-11-04 (Linux CI removed; GCC 13 and
  Clang 18 with sanitizers and fuzzing last passed at `10bfe03`, except a
  gcc-avx2 NLopt configure failure); Windows (Visual Studio 2022 / MSVC, AVX2;
  CI job `msvc-windows`: the AVX2 build's full test suite, demos, latency
  probe, and an AVX-512 compile; the AVX-512 build passed 285/285 on a
  Ryzen 9 9900X under Windows Server 2025, where `train_ensemble`
  reproduced E25's 97.8% in 141 s). Build steps and measured
  latency for Windows: `docs/WINDOWS.md`.
* **RAG engine:**
  * hybrid retrieval in four dense modes;
  * zero-allocation queries;
  * batched queries, bit-identical to single queries;
  * concurrent ingest, commit and query, verified under TSan and with
    `rag_stress`;
  * persistence, with checksum plus structural validation and crash-safe
    saves;
  * PDF ingestion with page citations.
* **Self-learning agent:**
  * model-free learners: tile-coded Q-learning with 3-step returns, and
    episodic memory;
  * a model-based agent (grey-box regression plus DP planning) at 99–100%
    of the optimum after 20–50 episodes;
  * an exact DP yardstick, also for generalized cost tables;
  * held-out evaluation and champion/challenger deployment;
  * two-stage drift detection with re-exploration, plus a change check at
    every evaluation that also catches markets that got better;
  * a policy bank that recognizes returning regimes;
  * an actor-critic learner (the best model-free one here) and risk-averse
    (mean-variance) objectives with an exact optimum;
  * an optional baseline guard: never deploy a policy that hasn't beaten
    TWAP;
  * a paired model check that flags a wrong model after 100 episodes, and
    warm-starting a model-free learner from a model's plan;
  * change detection for the model-based agent: recovery within 20
    episodes of a market flip, free while the market is stable;
  * off-policy evaluation (importance sampling, doubly robust, ESS
    diagnostics) and exact policy evaluation under a model;
  * a second task (market making, exact DP) on which the unchanged agent
    learns the optimal inventory skew, and a model-based market maker;
  * a from-scratch neural Q-learner (`NeuralQ`, no ML library) that breaks
    the tile-coding plateau on execution (91.5% [90.1, 92.7] at 40k
    episodes with Double DQN, 8 seeds; E21 ranks every learner);
  * `ExecutionPolicyTable`: any execution policy compiled into a lookup
    table (3 ns per decision, the network's gain within 0.25 points), and
    a median vote of 8 seeds' tables at 97.5% of the optimum (E22, E23);
  * optional NLopt/Eigen tooling: hyperparameter search validated on
    held-out seeds, sensitivity analysis, rank-revealing least squares.
* **Reviews:** four review agents (adversarial bug hunt, coverage, RAG
  property tests, and a review plus fuzzer for `policy_table.hpp`) found
  and fixed 17 bugs, six of them in the engine (including a heap overflow
  reachable through config and through a forged index file, R13–R17) and
  three in the policy table (A46); agent-header coverage is 99.7% of lines and 99.0% of
  regions. Their schedule fixes may shift the online-gain numbers of E8,
  E11 and E15 slightly; those were measured before the fixes.
* **Testing:**
  * the full suite runs on the user's AVX-512 server and in CI's
    `msvc-windows` (the sanitizer and fuzz jobs were Linux-only and are gone);
  * 287 ctest cases: 272 in `rag_tests`, 4 in `rag_optlib_tests`, 9 PDF tests (Poppler), the
    stress run and a `rag_pdf` CLI smoke test. With `RAG_PYTHON` ON (as in
    `build-avx512`) ctest also runs `ragrl_pytest` and 9 `ragrl_smoke_*`
    tests: 379/379 passed on 2026-11-12; the kit's fast gate runs the
    ragrl pytest suite (not the smokes).
  * The TSan CI job skips the single-threaded learning suites (`Agent*`,
    `ModelBased`, `ExecutionCosts`, `OffPolicy`, `MarketMaking`, `Reference`, `ApiExamples`), which ran
    for over an hour under TSan and have no threads to check.

## Known limitations

* **No neural embedder.** `HashingEmbedder` is lexical-ish (feature-hashed
  unigrams and bigrams). The `Embedder` concept is ready for ONNX or
  llama.cpp encoders, but none could be fetched or tested here (Hugging Face
  is blocked by this environment's network policy).
* **No LLM call.** The engine produces prompts; `Generator` is a concept
  only.
* **`commit()` rebuilds BM25 (and IVF) from scratch:** O(total corpus) per
  commit. Incremental segments would be the scalable design.
* **Capacity is fixed** at construction (`max_chunks`), and there are no
  deletes or updates of chunks.
* **PDF scope:** no OCR for scanned pages, and encrypted PDFs are rejected.
* **Agent:**
  * model-free learning on execution is slow because returns are noisy
    relative to the gain (0.27 return-sds per episode): default TileQ
    reaches 65–69% at 40k episodes; coarse tiles + advantage learning
    76–80%; the neural `NeuralQ` with Double DQN 91.5% [90.1, 92.7]
    (8 seeds, E21); the model-based
    agent 99–100% in 20–50 episodes (E1, E16, E19);
  * `ExecutionEnv` and `MarketMakingEnv` are stylized on purpose (exact DP
    optima): no order-book dynamics, queue position or real data;
  * `NeuralQ` is CPU-only and unvectorized (1.7 µs per decision).
* **Optional tooling:** tuning and analysis need NLopt and Eigen
  (`RAG_OPTLIBS`); without them those targets are skipped.
* **Machine-specific numbers:** benchmarks come from one shared 4-vCPU VM.

## Open questions and suspicions (from the review agents, not confirmed as bugs)

* `simd::quantize_i8`: a vector whose largest component is below ~3.7e-37
  overflows the scale (unreachable for unit-norm embeddings).
* `IvfIndex::load` checks row ids are in range but not unique; a forged
  file can repeat ids (duplicate hits, no memory-safety issue).
* IVF-SQ8 reranks committed rows with `simd::dot` but uncommitted rows with
  `dot1`: a row can score an ulp differently before and after `commit()`.
* `RrfParams` and `CleanOptions::repeated_line_fraction` aren't validated
  (NaN or negative values lead to an undefined float-to-int cast).
* `DriftDetector::rebase()` counts the held samples twice in the new
  baseline (slower adaptation, no failing scenario found).
* With `keep_champion = false`, the baseline guard checks the champion but
  deploys the learner.
* `MarketMakingEnv::reset()` doesn't clear `last_bought`/`last_sold`/
  `last_mid_move`; `ExecutionEnv::features` divides by `signal_sd`.
* The online-gain numbers of E8, E11 and E15 were measured before the
  re-plan schedule fix (A40) and may shift slightly.
* OF15's 0xc0000374 heap corruption: 5 worker crashes on 2026-11-05
  (~0.15% of evaluations, all at `run_policy` teardown), none in ~1,450
  LightGBM evaluations under `HeapValidate` and ASan on 2026-11-12. Not
  root-caused; leading hypothesis Python's `_wmi.exec_query` timeout path.
  Settling it needs WMI repaired and ~150 lgbm seed-runs with the bypass
  off and on (EXPERIMENTS.md OF15).

## Next steps (in rough priority order)

0. **ES order-flow trading** (the current focus): the ranked plan is in
   `ORDERFLOW.md` §7. After OF15 (no RL edge on either simulator, through
   a leak-proof pipeline) and OF16 (the 16 OF15 runs scored once on 8 real
   ES test days: no edge, best t 1.2 against 4.63; `OF16_REAL_ES_PLAN.md`,
   EXPERIMENTS.md OF16 phase 2), the simulator-trained RL line is closed
   (A79). OF17 (a census of the agents' 13 features on the 8 real days)
   found nothing that pays a market-order round trip at 1 s to 1 min, and
   none detected at 2-10 min, where the features predict within a month
   with a sign that flips (A82). OF18 tested that regime question on 228
   days of `.scid` trades: no walk-forward regime rule pays (A83). Market
   orders on order-flow and trend features are closed from 1 s to 15 min.
   OF19 (footprint patterns on 47 days of true tick data: stacks,
   absorption, exhaustion, unfinished auctions, trapped traders) found no
   edge either (A85). OF20 tested absorption and exhaustion only at
   key levels (prior session, overnight, initial balance, VWAP bands): no
   edge, and no better than a plain level touch (A86). OF21 pre-registers
   a forward test of exhaustion at those levels on days from 2026-11-12
   (looks at 60 / 125 / 250 full days, about Feb 2027 to late 2027; low
   power, A87); Sierra must record ESH27 tick by tick by 2026-12-04.
   Next (the user's choice): passive execution, which needs queue data
   (MBO) to replay honestly, or other information; the heap crash once WMI is
   repaired; then calibration to the user's ES ticks and the tick scalper
   at 100 ms latency on fresh seeds.

1. A neural `Embedder` (e.g. ONNX Runtime with a MiniLM model, 384 dims to
   match), on a machine that can download models.
2. Incremental BM25 segments with background merging.
3. Agent: Q-value averaging across seeds (vote on values, not actions;
   RESEARCH.md §6.4); test the hint that the networks over-trade (E23);
   a policy table for market making. (Weight averaging: done, E24;
   averaged networks voted: E25.)
4. Agent: the research's remaining ideas (RESEARCH.md §5): all-action
   counterfactual updates, value/advantage sharing across actions,
   Munchausen targets, weight averaging for the deployed policy.
5. Tune `NeuralQ` and actor-critic with `tune_agent`'s machinery (needs a
   larger compute budget: one NeuralQ evaluation is ~75 s per seed).
6. A richer market simulator (queue position, order book) for both tasks.
7. Resolve the open questions above.
