## FILE: docs/ai/OF18_REGIME_PLAN.md  (9,950 bytes)

# OF18 plan: month-scale momentum and reversal regimes in ES (5-30 min)

Status: **done 2026-11-12: no primary edge, no secondary candidate** (EXPERIMENTS.md OF18, `experiments/of18_regimes.txt`; upper bounds 1.55 / 2.53 / 3.46 ticks per trade at 5 / 10 / 15 min, 0.85 for the primary). Originally: plan only, nothing run. Written 2026-11-12 after OF17 (A82),
revised the same day after an independent design review (before any OF18
number existed). Committing this file is the pre-registration: data,
features, rules, statistics and the decision rule are fixed here.

## 1. Question

OF17 found one lead. At 2-10 min, trend features predict the ES move
within a month, with a sign that flips between months. The clearest case
is vwap_dist at 600 s: the top-minus-bottom quintile move was +10.0 ticks
in November 2026 (momentum) and −7.3 in September (reversal). Eight days
could not say more. The question here:

**Is the sign of that relationship persistent enough that a rule
estimating it from the last few weeks, and trading it the next day, beats
a market-order round trip?**

One **primary** test aims at the lead itself (§5); 63 **secondary** rules
map the neighbourhood at a multiplicity-adjusted bar. Expected answer: no.
Intraday momentum and reversal in index futures are weak and unstable,
and the OF17 reviewer's walk-forward variant (direction from the previous
day) reached t 1.8.

**Power, stated before the results** (the design review's estimate, 1-min
SD ~9 ticks, ~40% of decisions trading): the smallest net per trade a
secondary rule can detect is about 1.5-1.9 ticks at 5 min, ~2.7 at 10 min,
~3.9 at 15 min; the primary test (t ≥ 2) about 1.1 ticks at 10 min. OF17's
lead implies at most ~4-5 ticks gross per trade at 10 min if the sign were
always right, so a null result here is an upper bound, never "no regimes".

## 2. Data

The user's Sierra `.scid` trade files (read only; nothing enters the repo):
ESH26, ESM26, ESU26, ESZ26 in `C:\SierraChart\Data`. Surveyed 2026-11-12:
records are 1-s bundles (open, high, low, close of trade prices, trades,
volume, sell-aggressor "bid" volume, buy-aggressor "ask" volume) through
August 2026, and tick by tick from early September 2026, where each
record's high is the ask, its low the bid and its close the trade price.

- **Days:** every RTH day (08:30-15:00 CT, the OF16 clock with the explicit
  US DST rule) from **2025-12-12 to 2026-11-11**. 2025-12-12 is the day
  after the CME roll date for ESZ25 (2025-12-11), the first day ESH26 is
  the front month; there is no ESZ25 file, so earlier days would be the
  back month. From then on the front month is the file with the larger
  RTH volume that day (the OF13 rule; both contracts of each later roll
  have files). **Nothing from 2026-11-12 on is read** (the confirm block;
  the bar builder refuses those days).
- **Bars:** 1-min bars built from the records by a new tool, one file per
  day in `build-avx512/real/bars/` (gitignored) with the file's sha256 in a
  sidecar. For 1-s bundles: the first bundle's open, the maximum high, the
  minimum low, the last bundle's close (a bundle never spans a minute
  boundary). For tick records: open, high, low and close all from the
  records' close field (the trade price). Volume = the sum of volume; buy
  aggressor = ask volume, sell aggressor = bid volume.
- **Outages:** a minute with zero volume is a gap. A decision is skipped if
  any minute of its feature window (the 30 min before it) or of its
  holding period is a gap; a day with more than 5 gap minutes is dropped
  and listed. Early closes drop out by the same rule.
- **Amendment 2026-11-12 (bars built, before any OF18 statistic).** The
  bars show ESH26 at 150,046 RTH lots on 2025-12-12 against 847,556 to
  1,664,448 on 12-15..12-18, and the March roll's volume crossing on the
  Monday after the CME roll date (03-16): 12-12 was still the back month
  by volume, the rule above. The span starts **2025-12-15**. Built: 236
  weekdays with trades; 7 holiday or half sessions have 165-180 gap
  minutes and drop out; 228 used days; 0 OHLC violations; tick records
  from 2026-09-08.
- The 13 OF16 days (8 seen, 5 dev) are inside the span. They helped form
  the hypothesis, so a sensitivity run without them is reported (§5).

## 3. Decisions, features, outcomes

Bars are numbered 1..390 (bar 1 = 08:30-08:31 CT); close(0) = open of
bar 1.

**Decisions** at the end of bars m = 30, 35, 40, ... while m + 1 + h ≤ 390:
71, 70, 69 and 66 decisions a day for h = 5, 10, 15 and 30 min.

**Features** at a decision (bars 1..m of the day only), 7 in all:
- ret_L = close(m) − close(m − L), L = 5, 15, 30 min;
- delta_L = Σ (buy − sell aggressor volume) over bars m − L + 1..m,
  L = 5, 15, 30;
- vwap_dist = close(m) − session VWAP, the VWAP over bars 1..m weighted by
  volume at each bar's (high + low + close) / 3.

**Outcomes** at h = 5, 10, 15 (tested) and 30 min (descriptive only), in
ticks: entry at the open of bar m + 1 (the first trade after the
decision), exit at the open of bar m + 1 + h; move = exit − entry. A
trade nets side × move − **1.28 ticks** (OF17's measured mean round trip on
the front month: the recorded spreads at entry and exit plus $1.50 per
side). Stress: 1.50 ticks. Nothing crosses a day.

## 4. Rules (walk-forward by day)

For a feature, a horizon h and a lookback W (trading days), on each scored
day D:
- from the W used days before D only: the feature's 20th and 80th
  percentiles, and the direction d = sign(mean move at h in the top
  quintile − mean move in the bottom quintile);
- on D: when d = +1, long above the 80th percentile and short below the
  20th; when d = −1 the reverse; when d = 0, no trade.

**Primary:** vwap_dist, h = 10 min, W = 20 days.
**Secondary:** the 7 features × h ∈ {5, 10, 15} × W ∈ {5, 20, 60} = 63
rules (the primary is one of them).
**Descriptive:** the same at h = 30 (21 rules), not tested.

Scored days: every used day with at least 60 used days before it, the same
days for every rule, about 165. Nothing is tuned.

## 5. Statistics and the decision rule (fixed now)

Per rule over the scored days: trades, mean gross and mean net per trade
(total net / total trades), the first-half and second-half net (split at
the middle scored day), and the SE: **the largest of the moving-block
bootstrap SEs with blocks of 5, 10 and 21 consecutive scored days**
(B = 10000, seed 0; each resample's statistic is total net / total trades
over the resampled days), since a regime that persists makes a
walk-forward rule's days correlated for up to the regime's length.
t = mean net / SE.

**Bias check (binding).** On the scored days from 2026-09-08 on, which
have bid and ask in the records, every trade is also priced at the mid
(the mean of the ask and bid in the record of the entry and exit trades):
bias = side × [(exit trade − exit mid) − (entry trade − entry mid)], per
trade, averaged per rule and reported separately for rules whose d agrees
with the feature's sign (momentum) and disagrees (reversal). A rule's
net after bias = mean net − its mean bias.

- **Primary edge:** net > 0, t ≥ 2, positive in both halves, positive at
  the stress cost and after its bias.
- **Secondary candidate:** the same with t ≥ t_req = 2 + sqrt(2 ln 63) =
  4.88.
- Either goes to **OF18b**, one confirmation on the next 40 full
  front-month RTH days from 2026-11-12 (about two months of recording):
  the rule frozen and still walk-forward, each trade at the recorded touch
  (buy at the ask, sell at the bid, plus $1.50 per side), the SE the
  largest of 5- and 10-day block bootstraps; claimed only if net > 0 with
  t ≥ 2.
- **Otherwise the conclusion is an upper bound:** "No walk-forward regime
  rule on these 7 trend features nets more than X_h ticks per trade at h"
  (X_h = the median t_req × SE over the rules at h, and 2 × SE for the
  primary), "smaller edges are not excluded."

**Reported, not tested:**
- **Persistence**, apart from any rule: per feature, h and W, the
  correlation over scored days between the trailing-W estimate of the
  top-minus-bottom quintile move and day D's own value, with the
  block-bootstrap SE (largest of 5, 10, 21). This is the regime measured
  directly.
- Power: X_h per h.
- Trades by time of day (the percentiles are pooled over the day, so
  trades may cluster after the open).
- Sensitivity: the primary and the 5 best secondary rules without the 13
  OF16 days.

**Red flags** (stop and check before believing): t > 8, or gross above
3 ticks per trade at h = 5 min (a candidate at 5 min would be near this
size; check before believing it): check that features use bars 1..m only
and the entry is bar m + 1's open. The bar builder's daily volume must
equal the sum of the records' volume. Every bar must have high ≥ max(open,
close) and low ≤ min(open, close), and the distribution of bar-to-bar
price changes must not shift at the switch from bundles to ticks (early
September).

## 6. Known weaknesses (stated before the results)

- **Power.** See §1. A null is an upper bound.
- **Trade prices, not quotes.** Through August, entries and exits are
  trade prices with a constant cost; the bias check measures the effect
  where quotes exist (about 45 scored days) and binds a candidate to it.
- **One year, one market.** About 165 scored days and a few regime
  periods; the block bootstrap only partly reflects how few.
- **Overlap.** At h = 15, decisions 5 min apart overlap three-fold; the SE
  comes from day blocks, not trade counts.
- **Features.** Seven trade-based trend features, no book (OF17 covered
  the book features up to 10 min on 8 days).
- **Market orders only.** No passive execution, no sizing.

## 7. Cost

Light. The bar builder reads each day's byte range of the `.scid` files
once (about 3 GB, the tick-by-tick months most of it) and caches small bar
files; the statistics are seconds of numpy (the bootstraps a few
minutes). Implementation and review: about 1.5-2 hours.



---

## FILE: docs/ai/OF19_FOOTPRINT_PLAN.md  (11,469 bytes)

# OF19 plan: footprint order-flow patterns on true tick data

Status: **done 2026-11-12: no primary edge, no secondary candidate** (EXPERIMENTS.md OF19, `experiments/of19_footprint.txt`). Originally: plan only, nothing run. Written 2026-11-12 (revised the same day after an
independent design review, before any OF19 number) at the user's
request ("work on orderflow trends, exhaustion, absorptions") after OF18.
Committing this file is the pre-registration: data, pattern definitions,
directions, exits, statistics and the decision rule are fixed here before
any OF19 number is computed.

## 1. Question and what is already known

Do the classic footprint patterns (stacked imbalances, absorption,
exhaustion, unfinished auctions, trapped traders) make money on ES after
the real costs, when they are measured on true tick data with the
aggressor side and the bid and ask of every trade?

Already known (the user's knowledge base, `SierraChart-KB/reports/ES
order flow strategies.md`, `performance/orderflow-models/evidence/
strategies.txt`): the bar-level rules were tested out of sample on ESU26 /
ESZ26 at 1.4 ticks per round trip and all lost (delta divergence DD1-DD3
−2.2 to −2.7 ticks per trade, t down to −8.8; exhaustion fade S2 −1.8 to
−3.0; effort-vs-result absorption VA2 −1.68). Those bar-total rules are
exact on 1-s records and are **not retested** here. The per-price rules
(stacks, exhaustion at the extreme, unfinished auction, absorption at a
price) were only approximated there, from 1-s records in which most
volume spans several prices, and the KB's own verdict was to give them
"one clean run on true tick data". OF19 is that run. OF17 and OF18 (A82,
A83) found nothing in order-flow and trend features from 1 s to 15 min.

Expected answer: no.

## 2. Data

The user's Sierra `.scid` tick records (one record per trade, with the
trade price, size, aggressor side and the bid and ask at the trade) on the
front month by RTH volume: the **47** RTH days 2026-09-08 .. 2026-11-11
(ESU26 to 09-11, ESZ26 from 09-14; the OF18 bars' tick days, none with a
gap). **Nothing from 2026-11-12 on is read** (the confirm block). A new
tool writes per-day arrays of the RTH trades (time, price, size, side,
bid, ask) to `build-avx512/real/trades/` (gitignored, sha256 in a
sidecar), checking that the day's volume equals the OF18 bar file's.
A record with both bid and ask volume is split into two trades. The 8
seen OF16 days are inside the span; this study makes no claim on any day
of it (a candidate goes to fresh days, §5).

**Warm-up:** the first 10 days (to 2026-09-21) only feed the trailing
thresholds; **scored days are the other 37** (2026-09-22 .. 2026-11-11).
Every threshold that is a quantile comes from the 10 used days before the
day it is applied to.

Footprint bars: 1-min RTH bars (bar 1 = 08:30-08:31 CT). A(p) = buy-aggressor
volume traded at price p in the bar, Bv(p) = sell-aggressor volume. Lookbacks
use only bars of the same RTH session: a pattern needing N prior bars cannot
fire on bars 1..N.

**Quote check (before any statistic).** The share of buy-aggressor trades
priced at the record's ask and of sell-aggressor trades at its bid
(expected >= 95%; the OF18 review found ~2-3% of trades outside the quote,
stale quotes), and the count of records with bid >= ask or a zero side;
those use the last valid quote. Reported with the results; a share below
90% stops the study.

## 3. Patterns (direction fixed by the practitioner rule; long side shown, short is the mirror)

Primary settings are the user's Sierra study defaults where they exist
(`orderflow-footprint/footprint_core.hpp`: ratio 3, min volume 10,
stack 3, exhaustion threshold 5), else the KB's rule F1-F7
(`practitioner_methods.md`). Two stated deviations: Bv(p − 1) = 0 breaks a
stack (the study's rule; F1's vmin2 branch is not used), and P3's monotone
condition is from F3 (a), not the study.

**Signal time.** For bar-close patterns (P1, P4, P5) it is the bar's end
boundary (e.g. 08:31:00.000 CT), not its last trade. For P2 and P3 it is
the trigger trade's time.

- **P1 stacked imbalance (F1 a).** Diagonal buy imbalance at p:
  A(p) ≥ r · Bv(p − 1 tick), A(p) ≥ 10 and Bv(p − 1 tick) > 0. A buy stack is
  k consecutive prices with a buy imbalance. A bar with a buy stack and no
  sell stack: **long** at its close. Stop 2 ticks below the stack's lowest
  imbalance price. No target. Primary r = 3, k = 3.
- **P2 absorption (F2).** Seconds t are scanned in order; P = the bid of
  the last trade at or before t; S = sell-aggressor volume traded at P in
  (t − W, t]. A candidate when S ≥ the q95 of the same statistic (sampled
  every second, same 30-min time-of-day bucket, trailing 10 days) and no
  trade at or below P − 1 tick in (t − W, t]. It is confirmed if no trade
  at or below P − 1 prints in the next 10 s (the signal time is t + 10 s);
  a candidate that fails confirmation does not use up the slot. After a
  confirmed signal at t, the next t considered on that side is t + W.
  Trigger: **long** at the first trade at or above P + 1 tick within 60 s
  after the signal time; the pending entry is cancelled if a trade prints
  at or below the stop (P − 2 ticks) first. Target 4 ticks above the entry
  fill. Primary W = 30 s, target 4.
- **P3 exhaustion, sense (a) (F3 a, the footprint study's sense).** A bar
  makes a new low against the previous N bars, Bv(low) ≤ e and
  Bv(low) < Bv(low + 1 tick) < Bv(low + 2 ticks) (sellers dry up into the
  low). Trigger: **long** at the first trade above the bar's high within
  the next 5 bars, cancelled if a trade prints at or below the stop (1
  tick below the bar's low) first. No target. Primary N = 1, e = 5.
- **P4 unfinished auction (F4, PR3 trade).** The bar's high has A(H) > 0
  and Bv(H) > 0 and the bar closes at least d ticks below H: **long** at
  its close, target H (the revisit), stop 8 ticks below the entry fill. A
  bar unfinished at both extremes, each at least d from the close, is
  skipped. Primary d = 4.
- **P5 trapped traders (F7).** Bar t breaks below the 20-bar low L with
  the trigger, and one of bars t+1..t+M closes back above L: **long** at
  that close; stop 1 tick below bar t's low. No target. Triggers: *stack*
  = a sell stack (P1's rule, r = 3, k = 3) whose lowest price is in bar
  t's bottom 3 ticks; *delta* = bar t's delta ≤ the q10 of bar delta
  (trailing 10 days, same 30-min bucket; a bar-total rule like those §1
  does not retest, kept as a variant). Primary M = 2, trigger stack.

**Fills and exits (all patterns).** Records sharing one timestamp form
one event (a sweep). Entry: the first event at or after the signal time
+ 100 ms (for P2 and P3, the trigger trade's event + 100 ms); a long
fills at the worst (highest) of the event's asks and trade prices, a
short at the lowest of its bids and trade prices. A signal whose entry
fill is at or beyond its stop is skipped and counted. Stops, targets and
the time cap are checked on events after the entry event. Stop: the first
event with a trade at or through the stop; a long exits at the lowest of
that event's bids and trade prices. Target: a resting limit, filled only
when a trade prints one tick through it, at the target price. Otherwise
exit at the bid of the first event after the time cap: **15 min** (P2:
5 min), or at the bid of the last RTH trade before 15:00:00 CT. Fees $1.50
per side (0.24 ticks per round trip). Positions of a rule may overlap
(per-trade expectancy, not a strategy with position limits).

## 4. Tests

- **Primary:** the 5 patterns at their primary settings, M = 5,
  t_req = 2 + sqrt(2 ln 5) = 3.79.
- **Secondary** (the primaries included), 20 rules, t_req = 2 +
  sqrt(2 ln 20) = 4.45: P1 r ∈ {3, 4} × k ∈ {3, 4}; P2 W ∈ {15, 30, 60} s
  × target ∈ {4, 8}; P3 N ∈ {1, 20} × e ∈ {0, 5}; P4 d ∈ {4, 8}; P5
  M ∈ {1, 2} × trigger ∈ {stack, delta}.

Long and short signals of a rule are pooled. Nothing is tuned.

## 5. Statistics and the decision rule (fixed now)

Per rule over the 37 scored days: trades, skipped signals, win rate, mean
gross and mean net per trade (ticks, at the recorded touch, net of fees),
the first- and second-half net (split at the middle scored day), and the
SE: the largest moving-block bootstrap SE over blocks of 2, 5 and 10
scored days (10000 resamples, seed 0; statistic total net / total
trades). t = net / SE. Stress: net − 0.5 ticks (half a tick more slippage
per round trip).

- **Primary edge:** net > 0, t ≥ 3.79, positive in both halves and at
  stress. **Secondary candidate:** the same with t ≥ 4.45.
- Either goes to **OF19b**: one confirmation on the next 20 full
  front-month RTH days from 2026-11-12 (about four weeks of recording),
  rule frozen, thresholds still trailing; claimed only if net > 0 with
  t ≥ 2.
- **Otherwise:** "No footprint pattern at these settings nets more than
  X ticks per trade" (X = t_req × SE per rule, reported), "smaller edges
  are not excluded."

**Reported, not tested:**
- **Shell control** (does the footprint condition add anything?): each
  rule without its footprint condition, same entries and exits: P2
  without S ≥ q95; P3 a new N-bar low, then a break above the bar's high,
  without the Bv conditions; P4 finished highs (A(H) = 0 or Bv(H) = 0) at
  the same d; P5 break and reclaim without the trigger. P1 has no shell
  (its condition is the whole pattern) and is compared with the random
  control only. Rule gross minus shell gross, with the block SE of the
  difference.
- **Random control:** K = 10 random seconds per signal trade, same day,
  30-min bucket and side (seed 0); each uses the signal trade's stop and
  target as tick distances from its own entry fill and the same cap.
  Rule gross minus control gross, with its block SE.
- **Touch fills:** P2 and P4 with targets filled on touch (an optimistic
  bound).
- **Revisit rates** (F4's benchmark): the share of unfinished and of
  finished highs and lows revisited within 15 and 30 min, at the same
  distance d from the close.
- Trades by time of day; long and short separately.
- **Power:** X per rule.

**Red flags** (stop and check before believing): t > 6; a win rate above
75% on 50+ trades; gross above 3 ticks on trades held under a minute;
check that no record after the signal time (plus 100 ms) is used for the
entry decision, and that the day's trade volume equals the OF18 bars'.

## 6. Known weaknesses (stated before the results)

- **Few days.** 37 scored days, two months, two contracts. P5 is expected
  to fire a few times a day; its X will be several ticks and a null on it
  says little. Rough expectation (the review's, before any data): P4 tens
  to 100+ trades a day, P1 and P3 tens.
- **No targets for P1, P3, P5** (the practitioner rules give none): they
  exit on the stop or the time cap only.
- **Queue position.** Targets fill only on a trade-through, which is
  conservative and penalises P4's revisit thesis (the touch-fill bound is
  reported); stops and entries are market orders at the recorded touch,
  sweep-aware. 1 lot, no impact.
- **Levels.** Practitioners trade these patterns "at key levels" with
  discretion; this tests them everywhere they occur.
- **No book.** Absorption without the displayed bid size (the optional
  refill condition needs depth, recorded on only ~13 days).

## 7. Cost

Light: one pass over ~47 days of tick records (the ESU26 / ESZ26 byte
ranges, ~1.5 GB), per-day arrays, then numpy. Implementation, tests and
review: about 2-3 hours.



---

## FILE: docs/ai/OF20_LEVELS_PLAN.md  (8,010 bytes)

# OF20 plan: absorption and exhaustion at key levels

Status: **done 2026-11-12: no edge** (EXPERIMENTS.md OF20, `experiments/of20_atlevels.txt`). Originally: plan only, nothing run. Written 2026-11-12 after OF19 (A85),
at the user's request; revised the same day after an independent design
review, before any outcome. Committing this file is the pre-registration:
levels, setups, statistics and the decision rule are fixed here before
any OF20 profit or loss is computed.

## 1. Question

Practitioners say absorption and exhaustion work "at a key level", not
everywhere (the user's KB, `practitioner_methods.md` F2, and its section
6: absorption's discretionary component is "at a key level"). OF19 tested
them everywhere and found no edge. The question here:

**At the standard auction-market levels, do the absorption and exhaustion
setups make money after costs, and do they beat a plain touch of the same
level without the footprint condition?**

What was looked at before writing this: OF19's results for these rules
over all occurrences (all negative), and the **counts** (not the outcomes)
of their signals near the levels below on the 37 OF19 scored days
(absorption W 15: 44 within 2 ticks of a static level, 91 of any level;
exhaustion N 1: 58 and 129). No outcome of any signal at a level has been
computed.

Expected answer: no. The test is coarse (§6).

## 2. Data and levels

The OF19 data: tick records of the front month, 47 days 2026-09-08 ..
2026-11-11; the same 37 scored days (2026-09-22 .. 2026-11-11), the first
10 days feeding the absorption threshold only. Nothing from 2026-11-12 on
is read. Levels (`experiments/of20_levels.py`, ticks), from data before
they are used:

- **Static** (known at 08:30 CT): the prior RTH session's high, low and
  close (its last trade), volume POC and 70% value area (from the POC, add
  the adjacent price with more volume, one tick at a time, ties above: a
  stated deviation from the classic two-row method, edges can differ by
  1-2 ticks); the overnight high and low (the same contract, 17:00 CT of
  the previous calendar day to 08:30 CT, the 08:30:00 print excluded); the
  initial balance high and low (08:30-09:30 CT RTH). RTH is 08:30-15:00 CT
  as in OF16-OF19 (not the 15:15 CT close some ES charts use). The prior
  session is the previous CME RTH session of day D's contract; on the 37
  scored days that is always the previous trades day (no gaps, no
  holidays).
- **VWAP set**: RTH VWAP and VWAP ± 1 and 2 volume-weighted SD, from RTH
  trades strictly before the formation time, each rounded to the nearest
  tick; active only from 09:30 CT (right after the open VWAP is about the
  price and the SD near 0, so every early signal would be "at VWAP").

**Formation time.** A level is judged at the setup's formation time:
absorption, the start of its window (t − W, with t the candidate second);
exhaustion, the start of its extreme bar. A level counts only if it was
known at that time: the initial balance only for setups formed at or after
09:30 CT, the VWAP set as above (none before the first trade).

A setup is **at a level** when its price is within `tol` ticks of a level
counted at its formation time: absorption, the absorbed price P;
exhaustion, the bar's extreme (the new low for a long, the new high for a
short).

## 3. Setups (OF19's definitions, exits and fills unchanged)

- **Absorption** = OF19 P2 with W = 15 s and target 4, at a level. W = 15
  was chosen for its count (the most frequent variant); it was also the
  least negative P2 variant in OF19 (net −1.66 against −2.10 for the
  primary), which was seen.
- **Exhaustion** = OF19 P3 with N = 1 and e = 5 (the study default), at a
  level.

Everything else is OF19 (§3 of `OF19_FOOTPRINT_PLAN.md`): trailing q95
for absorption, triggers, cancellation, stops, target, 15-min (P3) and
5-min (P2) caps, sweep-aware fills at the recorded touch, 100 ms latency,
$1.50 per side.

## 4. Tests

- **Primary (one test, t ≥ 2):** both setups pooled, at **any** level
  (static and VWAP sets), tol = 2 ticks: about 220 trades.
- **Secondary (8 tests, t_req = 2 + sqrt(2 ln 8) = 4.04):** each setup ×
  level set (static, VWAP) × tol ∈ {1, 2}.

## 5. Statistics and the decision rule (fixed now)

As OF19 §5: per-trade net at the recorded touch; SE the largest
moving-block bootstrap SE over blocks of 2, 5 and 10 scored days (10000
resamples, seed 0; total net / total trades); halves split at the middle
scored day; stress net − 0.5 ticks.

- **Edge:** net > 0, t ≥ its bar (2 primary, 4.04 secondary), positive in
  both halves and at stress, **and setup − shell ≥ 2 × the block SE of the
  difference** (the shell below). For the pooled primary, setup − shell =
  Σ_s w_s (setup_s − shell_s), with w_s the setup's share s of the
  primary's trades, its SE from resampling days jointly. Not a claim:
  OF20b, one confirmation on the next **40** full front-month RTH days
  from 2026-11-12 (the setups are rare: ~3-6 a day), claimed only if
  net > 0 with t ≥ 2 **and** setup − shell > 0. For OF20b the prior
  session is the previous recorded RTH session of the day's contract (read
  from that contract's .scid, so the December roll uses ESH27's own prior
  session); half days count as a prior session but are not scored; a day
  whose prior session is not recorded is skipped and counted.
- **If the setup passes net and t but not the shell condition**, the
  result reads "at these levels the touch, not the footprint condition,
  carries the result": the shell is not a pre-registered rule, so it is
  not a candidate and nothing is claimed.
- **Otherwise:** "At these levels the setups net no more than X ticks per
  trade (X = bar × SE)"; smaller edges are not excluded.

**The level-touch shell** (the comparison that answers the question): the
same setup without its footprint condition, at the same levels and
tolerance, with the same exits: absorption without S ≥ q95 (held price and
confirmation only); exhaustion as a new 1-bar low (high) then a break above
the bar's high (below its low), without the Bv (A) conditions. Reported
for every test: shell trades, shell net, and the setup minus the shell
with its block SE.

**Reported, not tested:** the interaction practitioners claim, (setup −
shell) at levels minus (setup − shell) away from all levels, with its block
SE; per level type (prior high/low, POC and value
area, overnight, initial balance, VWAP bands): trades and net; long and
short; trades by time of day; the absorption and exhaustion results away
from all levels (the complement), so the level effect is visible; X per
test.

**Red flags:** t > 6; a win rate above 75% on 30+ trades; a level computed
from data after its use (checked in code: static levels from the previous
day and the overnight before 08:30, the initial balance only after 09:30,
VWAP from trades before the signal time).

## 6. Known weaknesses (stated before the results)

- **Power.** About 220 trades in the primary over 37 days. Exhaustion's
  per-trade dispersion is about 3.5 times absorption's (OF19: SE 0.69 on
  788 trades against 0.23 on 574), so the pooled primary is mostly an
  exhaustion test: SE about 1.0, X about 2.1 ticks. The secondaries
  (25-60 trades) have X of roughly 4.5 (absorption) to 14 ticks
  (exhaustion): they cannot realistically pass, and a pass on 25 trades
  would itself be a red flag. A null says little below these sizes.
- **Seen data.** The same 37 days as OF19, whose overall results were
  seen. The at-level condition is the practitioners' a priori claim, not
  chosen from OF19's results, and only signal counts at levels were looked
  at.
- **Levels.** One standard set; practitioners also draw discretionary
  levels (prior swing points, composites, naked POCs, the official
  settlement rather than the last RTH trade) that are not tested.
- **No book.** As OF19.

## 7. Cost

Light: OF19's pass over the 47 days plus the levels (one overnight byte
range per day); minutes. Implementation, review: about 1.5-2 hours.



---

## FILE: docs/ai/OF21_FORWARD_PLAN.md  (12,640 bytes)

# OF21 plan: forward test of exhaustion at key levels

Status: **plan only, nothing run, no day on or after 2026-11-12 read.**
Written 2026-11-12 after OF20 (A86), at the user's request ("Yes,
pre-register it"); revised the same day after an independent design
review, before any forward day was read. Committing this file with the
frozen code (§8) is the pre-registration: the rule, the days, the looks,
the statistics and the decision rule are fixed here.

## 1. Question and the power warning

OF20 found no edge, but one descriptive line was positive: exhaustion
(OF19 P3, N 1, e 5) within 2 ticks of any OF20 level ("exh any tol 2"):
119 trades on 37 days, net +1.44 ticks per trade, block SE 2.17, median
−9.24, −0.96 without its top 3 trades, −0.68 without its best 2 days. It
was not a pre-registered test, it was the best-looking of the descriptive
rows, and it rests on a handful of trades: the in-sample +1.44 is very
probably inflated. The expected forward answer is **no**.

**Question:** on days nobody has seen, does exhaustion at the OF20 levels
make money after costs?

**Power (stated before any data, the main weakness).** The in-sample
per-day dispersion gives a net-per-trade SE of about 13.2 / sqrt(days):
2.1 at 40 days, 1.7 at 60, 1.2 at 125, 0.83 at 250. A fixed test at
t ≥ 2 with 80% power for the in-sample +1.44 would need about **680 full
days (2.7 years)**. Under the design below (final look at 250 days) the
power is 0.39 if the true edge is +1.44, 0.65 at +2, 0.94 at +3 and
≥ 0.99 at +4 ticks per trade (Monte Carlo, §4; the design review
recomputed these). So this test is likely to find only an edge about
twice the in-sample estimate or larger. A null at the final look is
reported as an upper bound (net + 2.007 × SE); 2.007 × SE (about 1.7 ticks
at 250 days) is the edge this test detects only about half the time.

## 2. Data and days

- **Source:** Sierra Chart tick-by-tick .scid records (`<q4f4I`, as
  OF19), read on or after 2026-11-12 only by `experiments/of21_forward.py`
  in `--count` or `--look` mode with the orchestrator flag. The converters
  `tools/scid_to_bars.py` and `tools/scid_to_trades.py` keep refusing those
  days, and `experiments/of20_levels.py`'s `overnight()` reads one only for
  a day this process opened as a real_forward look.
- **The scan** (`--count`, and the first step of a look) reads each
  forward day's RTH records for timestamps and volumes only (front month,
  full or not, a sha256 of the front month's overnight and RTH records), logs that per
  day in the ledger (block `real_forward_scan`) and prints only dates,
  front months and reasons: no prices, levels or outcomes.
- **Contracts:** ESZ26, ESH27, ESM27, ESU27, ESZ27, ESH28 (whichever files
  exist). Front month: the file with the larger RTH volume that day (OF13
  rule, `scid_to_bars.build_day`). **Sierra must record each new
  contract tick by tick before its roll window** (ESH27 by 2026-12-04: no
  ESH27 file exists today).
- **Calendar:** weekdays from 2026-11-12 on, in date order, only after
  their session is over. RTH 08:30-15:00 CT (the OF16 clock).
- **A full day** (scored), all of:
  - the front month's records are tick style (OF18 rule);
  - its RTH records cover the session: the first within 60 s of 08:30,
    the last within 60 s of 15:00, no gap between records over 60 s;
  - **roll window:** within 14 calendar days before the front month's
    expiry (the third Friday of its month), the next contract also has
    tick-style RTH records that day (so a missing file can never make an
    expiring contract the "front month");
  - the day's contract has a recorded prior session (below).
  Half days (2026-11-27, 2026-12-24, ...), recording outages and trading
  halts over 60 s are not scored; they are counted and reported with the
  reason. Dropping halt days drops some of the most extreme days: stated
  here as a weakness (§7), not corrected.
- **A day's status is fixed by its first scan** after its session ended
  (the earliest `real_forward_scan` entry). A later backfill cannot turn
  a not-full day into a full one. If the records of a full day change
  after its first scan (a different sha256), that is a red flag and every
  later look is refused until an amendment, committed before the look,
  says how to proceed.
- **Prior session:** the most recent earlier weekday (up to 7 calendar
  days back) on which the **day's own contract** has RTH records, full or
  not (a half day counts). On a roll day this is the new front month's
  own, thinner, prior session (as written in OF20 §5 for OF20b). The first
  forward day's prior session (2026-11-11) is before the cutoff and is read
  as a prior session only.
- **Overnight:** the same contract, 17:00 CT of the previous calendar day
  to 08:30 CT (OF20's `overnight()`, unchanged).

## 3. Rule (OF20, unchanged)

Exhaustion = OF19 P3 with N = 1, e = 5 (`sig_P3`), at a level: the bar's
extreme (the new low for a long, the new high for a short) within 2 ticks
of any level known at the setup's formation time (the start of its extreme
bar): the ten static levels (prior high, low, close, POC, 70% value area,
overnight high and low, initial balance from 09:30 CT) or the VWAP set
(VWAP ± 1 and 2 SD from 09:30 CT). Fills, exits, stops, the 15-min cap,
100 ms latency, $1.50 per side (0.24 ticks round trip on top of the
spread), sweep grouping: OF19 §3 unchanged. **Shell:** the same setup
without its footprint (Bv/A) conditions at the same levels (OF20 §5).
The trade simulation is the OF19/OF20 code, frozen (§8).

## 4. Looks

Three looks, each on the first N_k full scored days from 2026-11-12, in
date order, minus any day a `real_confirm` opening used (cumulative: look
2 contains look 1's days):

| look | full days N_k | about when | boundary (t ≥) | cumulative α spent (one-sided) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 60 | Feb 2027 | 4.504 | 0.0000033 |
| 2 | 125 | May 2027 | 3.022 | 0.0013 |
| 3 (final) | 250 | late 2027 | 2.007 | 0.0228 |

Lan-DeMets O'Brien-Fleming spending, α(τ) = 2 − 2Φ(z_{1−α/2}/sqrt(τ)) with
α = 1 − Φ(2) = 0.0228 (one-sided t ≥ 2) and τ = N_k / 250. Look 1's
boundary is exact (computed with erfc). Looks 2-3 come from 4,000,000
Monte Carlo Brownian paths (numpy, seed 0; correlations sqrt(N_j / N_k)),
Monte Carlo error about ±0.005 (the design review got 3.026 and 2.009).
The boundaries are constants in the runner. The bootstrap t is compared
with these normal boundaries, as OF19 and OF20 compared it with 2.

**Deadline.** If look 3's 250 days are not reached by **2028-03-31**,
look 3 is taken on that date or later on every full day so far (more than
look 2's days); its boundary then spends all α left (the runner's
`final_boundary`, the same Monte Carlo with the actual day counts). A
look not reached by the deadline is skipped.

Before a look, only the scan may run (§2). A look runs once the count
reaches N_k, on exactly the first N_k full days; it cannot be moved or
taken early. **Crash retry:** a look whose process died may be rerun only
with an identical fingerprint (the same days, the same per-day record
sha256 and the same code sha256); the output is deterministic (seed 0),
so a rerun reveals nothing new. It is logged as a retry.

## 5. Statistics and the decision rule (fixed now)

As OF20: per-trade net at the recorded touch; SE = the largest
moving-block bootstrap SE over blocks of 2, 5 and 10 scored days (10000
resamples, seed 0; statistic total net / total trades); stress = net −
0.5 ticks; setup − shell = setup gross per trade − shell gross per trade,
its SE by resampling days jointly; halves split at the look's middle
scored day.

At look k:
- **Edge (stop, then claim):** net > 0, t = net / SE ≥ the look's
  boundary, setup − shell > 0, and stress net > 0. A claim is only "the
  rule passed its pre-registered forward test"; trading it is a separate
  decision with its own costs and risk. Deliberate differences from
  OF20's in-sample rule: halves are not required, and setup − shell only
  needs to be positive, not 2 SE, because a forward test at this power
  cannot afford them. So a pass does **not** show that the footprint
  condition beats a plain touch of the level. The stress condition rarely
  binds once t clears the boundary; it stays for continuity.
- **Futility (looks 1-2, non-binding):** net ≤ 0. The result reads "no
  edge so far" and stopping is recommended; continuing is allowed because
  futility does not spend α (the boundaries assume no futility stop).
- **Otherwise:** continue to the next look. At the final look without an
  edge: "net N per trade, upper bound N + 2.007 × SE on fresh days; edges
  below 2.007 × SE are detected less than half the time".

**Reported at every look, not tested:** trades, win rate, median, net
without the top 3 trades and without the best 2 days (lesson 61), long
and short, halves, the shell's trades and net, trades per level type,
not-scored days with reasons, the front month per day, the code sha256.

**Red flags:** t > 6 at any look; a win rate above 75% on 30+ trades; any
level from data after its use (the OF20 checks); a look's days not the
first N_k full days; a full day whose records changed after its first
scan (§2); a frozen file whose sha256 differs from §8; the rehearsal not
reproducing OF20.

## 6. Ledger and guards

`ragrl.ledger.open_forward(claim, look, days, ...)` logs block
`real_forward` (claim `OF21`, look, days, fingerprint, the code and
manifest sha256) before any price of those days is read, and refuses: no
orchestrator flag; look k without look k−1; days that do not extend look
k−1's; any day an earlier `real_confirm` opening used; a look already
logged, except a crash retry with the same fingerprint (§4). Days read by
OF21 looks count as used for other confirmations
(`used_real_confirm_days`), so a later claim's confirmation takes other
days. `log_forward_scan` logs block `real_forward_scan` (§2).

Before every look, the runner (1) refuses unless every file in §8 has its
frozen sha256, (2) reruns the rehearsal (below) and refuses unless it
reproduces OF20 exactly, (3) scans and refuses on a changed full day. The
ledger and clock modules (`python/ragrl/ledger.py`, `python/ragrl/real.py`)
are not frozen (other work changes them); their sha256 is logged with
each look and the rehearsal checks the clock.

Usage (orchestrator only, through the kit):

    python experiments/of21_forward.py --count --i-am-the-orchestrator
    python experiments/of21_forward.py --look 1 --i-am-the-orchestrator --out experiments/of21_look1.txt

`--rehearse` reruns the same code path on OF20's 37 scored days
(2026-09-22 .. 2026-11-11, from the .scid files, no ledger) and must
reproduce OF20's "exh any tol 2" row exactly: 119 trades, net +1.44, SE
2.17, shell 925, setup − shell +2.61 ± 2.23. It did at the freeze
(`experiments/of21_rehearsal.txt`); all 37 days pass the full-day rule.

## 7. Known weaknesses

- **Power** (§1): only an edge of about 3 ticks per trade or more is
  likely to be found; the in-sample estimate is half that and inflated.
- **Selection:** the rule was the best-looking descriptive row of OF20;
  the forward days are what makes the test fair, nothing else.
- **Regime:** a year of new days is one market period; a pass would still
  need a check that it is not carried by a few trend days (reported).
- **Dropped days:** recording outages, missing contract files in a roll
  window and halt days are not scored (counted). Halt days are among the
  most extreme; excluding them can bias the result either way.
- **Costs:** market orders as in OF19; no queue position, no book.
- **The confirm block:** OF21 uses its days for about a year; another
  claim's confirmation in that time takes days OF21 has not yet read, and
  those days then leave OF21's sequence, so OF21's looks come later.

## 8. Frozen code

Every look refuses unless these files have exactly these sha256 (of the
file with CRLF read as LF; `python/tests/test_of21.py` fails on any
change):

- `experiments/of21_forward.py` sha256: `0adb0f61083aff1a38150b69e4d642b624fa26917d7a72be6268a07be80da0c7`
- `experiments/of20_atlevels.py` sha256: `075e691c368ec2002ff22b16b75792257aa068ace3e4ba0af23b9ab13572fcfc`
- `experiments/of20_levels.py` sha256: `7ec71ba08c38940226f898842219a6eb3f66648158d46374ec00d78bcc36690b`
- `experiments/of19_footprint.py` sha256: `47ca7794afedf6f81d99c634d216403a77a666c12c9a3b5b1ce95f2d0fe17470`
- `tools/scid_to_bars.py` sha256: `fd0f8118fc875b10bc0eddfa50596e58467fbc002f79516288a0079bf5b9eef3`
- `tools/scid_to_trades.py` sha256: `50b8c070043cc843a03d968254336159f1b19340896883b5c3792626bb4ba2e5`
